=== README.md ===


# Paper Digest Generator

Reduce token waste of paper digests by outsourcing the template to a JSON-into-HTML creation pipeline.

## Workflow

1. **Input** — HTML, PDF, or a link (arXiv / Semantic Scholar / any URL).
2. **Fetch** — links are resolved via the arXiv HTML version (`https://arxiv.org/html/<id>`) or fetched directly; raw HTML and local PDFs are accepted.
3. **Digest** — the source text goes to an OpenRouter agent governed by the master prompt (`src/prompt.js`). The agent returns a **JSON** digest (not HTML): verdict, motivation, core idea, distinguishing factor, evaluation, ICYMI, cautious judgement, sources/limits.
4. **Template** — the JSON digest is rendered into `assets/template.html` (self-contained Tailwind + MathJax + sentence-level reading marker) and written to `outputs/<Author-year-journalabbv-shortenedtitle.html>`.

## CLI

The CLI has three modes. `source` may be an arXiv id/link, a URL, raw HTML text, or a local PDF path.

### 1. `prompt` — emit the master prompt (no LLM)

```
paper-digest prompt <source> [--out <file>]
```

Fetches the source and prints the **master prompt** (the agent instructions that demand JSON output). Copy/paste this into any chat AI; the AI returns the digest JSON, which you then feed to `template`. Use this when you want a chat AI to do the digesting.

### 2. `digest` — full pipeline (fetch → OpenRouter agent → HTML)

```
paper-digest digest <source> [--out <dir>]
```

Fetches the source, runs the OpenRouter agent (`src/llm.js` follows the reference Python `LLM` class), parses the JSON digest, saves the intermediate `<name>.json`, and renders `<name>.html` to `--out` (default `outputs/`).

### 3. `template` — JSON already written, just render to HTML

```
paper-digest template [digest.json|-] [--out <dir>] [--stdout]
```

Takes an already-written digest JSON (e.g. produced by a chat AI from `prompt`) and renders it into the self-contained HTML template. No LLM call, no network fetch.

The JSON can come from:
- a file argument: `paper-digest template digest.json`
- stdin: `Get-Clipboard | paper-digest template` (writes HTML to `outputs/`)
- stdin with `--stdout`: `Get-Clipboard | paper-digest template --stdout | Set-Clipboard` (HTML straight to clipboard, no intermediate file)

Use `-` explicitly to read from stdin: `paper-digest template - --stdout`.

Output filename convention (all modes):

`Author-year-journalabbv-shortenedtitle.html`

(e.g. `Vaswani-2017-NeurIPS-attention_all_you_need.html`). Outputs are git-ignored.

## UI / preview

- `ui/index.html` — standalone browser UI. Paste a digest JSON (from `prompt` or a chat AI), tweak BASE URL / figures, and preview the rendered HTML. Copy or save the result.
- `ui/obsidian-dataviewjs.js` — self-contained Obsidian DataviewJS snippet. Drop it into a dataviewjs code block to render a digest JSON file inline in a vault note:

  ```dataviewjs
  let digestPath = dv.current().file.folder + "/Vaswani-2017-Neurips-attention_all_you_need.json";
  await renderPaperDigest(dv, container, digestPath);
  ```

  It embeds the same pure render logic as `src/render.js` (no Node, no network), so it works inside Obsidian.

## Configuration (OpenRouter)

The `digest` mode uses [OpenRouter](https://openrouter.ai). Provide an API key via one of:

- `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` environment variable, or
- `~/.paper_digest_key` file containing the key.

Configure models and limits in `paper-digest.config.json` (copy `paper-digest.config.json.example`):

```json
{
  "models": ["meta-llama/llama-3.2-3b-instruct"],
  "temperature": 0.2,
  "max_tokens": 2048,
  "retries": 2,
  "max_requests_per_run": 50,
  "timeout_s": 30
}
```

Env overrides: `PAPER_DIGEST_MODEL`. Models are tried in order; each is retried before falling back to the next. A 401 aborts immediately; 400/402/403/404 skip to the next model; 429 honors `Retry-After`.

Example:

```sh
export OPENROUTER_API_KEY=sk-or-...
paper-digest https://arxiv.org/abs/1706.03762 --out outputs
```

## Project structure

- `src/fetch.js` — source resolver (arXiv / URL / HTML / PDF) + figure map extraction
- `src/prompt.js` — master prompt; agent emits JSON
- `src/llm.js` — OpenRouter client (key loading, model fallbacks, retries, per-run cap)
- `src/digest.js` — agent orchestration
- `src/template.js` — JSON → HTML renderer
- `src/cli.js` — CLI entry point
- `assets/template.html` — self-contained digest template

<link href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=Literata:opsz,wght@7..72,400;7..72,600&family=Instrument+Sans:wght@400;600&display=swap" rel="stylesheet">
<script src="https://cdn.tailwindcss.com/3.4.17"></script>
<script>window.MathJax={tex:{inlineMath:[['\\(','\\)'],['$','$']],displayMath:[['\\[','\\]']]},svg:{fontCache:'global'}};</script>
<script async src="https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/mathjax@3.2.2/es5/tex-svg.js"></script>
<style>
:root{--paper:#F2F5F3;--ink:#16252C;--mute:#5B6C73;--rule:#CBD6D3;--teal:#0E6B73;--gain:#2B7A4B;--risk:#A83A2C;--warn:#9A6A12;--card:#FBFCFB}
body{background:var(--paper);color:var(--ink);font-family:Literata,Georgia,serif;line-height:1.65}
.ui{font-family:'Instrument Sans',system-ui,sans-serif}
h2.s{font-family:'Instrument Sans',system-ui,sans-serif;font-weight:600;font-size:1.35rem;border-left:4px solid var(--teal);padding-left:.7rem;margin:2.6rem 0 .8rem}
p,li{max-width:72ch}
ul.b{list-style:disc;padding-left:1.2rem;margin:.4rem 0}
.conf{font-family:'Instrument Sans',system-ui,sans-serif;font-size:.75rem;font-weight:600;color:var(--teal);border:1px solid var(--teal);border-radius:999px;padding:.05rem .5rem;margin-left:.5rem;white-space:nowrap;vertical-align:middle}
.chip{font-family:'Instrument Sans',system-ui,sans-serif;font-size:.72rem;font-weight:600;border-radius:4px;padding:.05rem .4rem;margin-right:.4rem;color:#fff}

<script>
const BASE = "{{BASE}}";
const MAP = {{MAP}};
document.querySelectorAll('figure[data-n]').forEach((f)=>{
  if (f.querySelector('img, svg')) return;
  const n=f.dataset.n,img=new Image();img.loading='lazy';img.alt='Figure '+n;
  img.onerror=()=>{const a=document.createElement('a');a.href=BASE.replace(/\/$/,'')+'#'+f.dataset.a;a.textContent='Open Fig. '+n+' on arXiv';a.className='ui text-sm underline';a.style.color='var(--teal)';img.replaceWith(a);};
  img.src=MAP[n] ? new URL(MAP[n], BASE || window.location.origin).href : "";
  f.prepend(img);
});
</script>
<script>
/* Reading marker. R or a click on a sentence starts it; Up/Down (or k/j) move it; Esc or R stops it.
   Arrow keys scroll the page normally unless the marker is active. Runs after MathJax has typeset. */
(function(){
const BLK='main p:not(.cite),main li:not(.cite),main .item,main blockquote,main figcaption,main td';
const ALL=BLK+',main mjx-container[display="true"]';
const ABBR=/(?:\b(?:e\.g|i\.e|et al|etc|vs|cf|Figs?|Eqs?|Secs?|Tab|Refs?|Algs?|approx|resp|No)|\b[A-Z])\.$/;
const OBJ='\uFFFC';
let units=[],idx=-1,on=false,ready=false,hintEl;

function tokens(block){
  const items=[];let run=[];
  const flush=()=>{if(run.length)items.push({run});run=[];};
  const walk=n=>{
    for(let c=n.firstChild;c;c=c.nextSibling){
      if(c.nodeType===3){run.push({t:c});}
      else if(c.nodeType===1){
        const tag=c.tagName;
        if(tag==='MJX-CONTAINER'){
          if(c.getAttribute('display')==='true'){flush();items.push({disp:c});}
          else run.push({a:c});
        }else if(tag==='SCRIPT'||tag==='STYLE'||tag==='MJX-ASSISTIVE-MML'||tag==='IMG'){}
        else walk(c);
      }
    }
  };
  walk(block);flush();return items;
}

function sentences(run){
  let s='';const map=[];
  for(const p of run){
    if(p.t){const v=p.t.nodeValue;for(let i=0;i<v.length;i++)map.push({n:p.t,o:i,L:v.length});s+=v;}
    else{map.push({a:p.a});s+=OBJ;}
  }
  const cuts=[0];
  const re=/[.!?]+["'’”)\]]*(?=\s+[A-Z0-9"'‘(\[\uFFFC])/g;
  let m;
  while((m=re.exec(s))){const end=m.index+m[0].length;if(!ABBR.test(s.slice(Math.max(0,end-10),end)))cuts.push(end);}
  cuts.push(s.length);
  const out=[];
  for(let i=0;i<cuts.length-1;i++){
    let a=cuts[i],b=cuts[i+1];
    while(a<b&&/\s/.test(s[a]))a++;
    while(b>a&&/\s/.test(s[b-1]))b--;
    if(b>a)out.push([a,b]);
  }
  return {out,map};
}

function rangeFor(block,map,a,b){
  const r=document.createRange(),f=map[a],l=map[b-1];
  if(f.a)r.setStartBefore(f.a);
  else if(f.o===0){let n=f.n;while(n.parentNode!==block&&n===n.parentNode.firstChild)n=n.parentNode;r.setStartBefore(n);}
  else r.setStart(f.n,f.o);
  if(l.a)r.setEndAfter(l.a);
  else if(l.o===l.L-1){let n=l.n;while(n.parentNode!==block&&n===n.parentNode.lastChild)n=n.parentNode;r.setEndAfter(n);}
  else r.setEnd(l.n,l.o+1);
  return r;
}

function build(block){
  const out=[];
  for(const it of tokens(block)){
    if(it.disp){it.disp.classList.add('rd');out.push(it.disp);continue;}
    const {out:ss,map}=sentences(it.run),spans=[];
    for(let i=ss.length-1;i>=0;i--){
      const r=rangeFor(block,map,ss[i][0],ss[i][1]);
      const sp=document.createElement('span');sp.className='rd';
      sp.appendChild(r.extractContents());r.insertNode(sp);spans.push(sp);
    }
    out.push(...spans.reverse());
  }
  return out;
}

function hint(){
  if(!hintEl){hintEl=document.createElement('div');hintEl.id='rd-hint';document.body.appendChild(hintEl);}
  hintEl.textContent=on?'\u2191 \u2193 move \u00b7 Esc exit':'R: reading marker';
}
function inView(i){const r=units[i].getBoundingClientRect();return r.bottom>0&&r.top<innerHeight;}
function firstVisible(){for(let i=0;i<units.length;i++)if(units[i].getBoundingClientRect().top>=48)return i;return units.length-1;}
function ensure(u,fast){
  const r=u.getBoundingClientRect(),h=innerHeight;
  if(r.top<h*.18||r.bottom>h*.78)scrollBy({top:r.top-h*.38,behavior:fast?'auto':'smooth'});
}
function set(i,scroll,fast){
  if(units[idx])units[idx].classList.remove('on');
  idx=Math.max(0,Math.min(units.length-1,i));
  units[idx].classList.add('on');
  if(scroll)ensure(units[idx],fast);
}
function activate(i){
  on=true;document.body.classList.add('rd-active');
  set(i!==undefined?i:(idx>=0&&inView(idx)?idx:firstVisible()),i===undefined);hint();
}
function deactivate(){
  on=false;document.body.classList.remove('rd-active');
  if(units[idx])units[idx].classList.remove('on');hint();
}

document.addEventListener('keydown',e=>{
  if(!units.length||e.ctrlKey||e.metaKey||e.altKey)return;
  const t=e.target;if(t&&(/^(INPUT|TEXTAREA|SELECT)$/.test(t.tagName)||t.isContentEditable))return;
  if(e.key==='r'||e.key==='R'){e.preventDefault();on?deactivate():activate();return;}
  if(!on)return;
  if(e.key==='ArrowDown'||e.key==='j'){e.preventDefault();set(idx+1,true,e.repeat);}
  else if(e.key==='ArrowUp'||e.key==='k'){e.preventDefault();set(idx-1,true,e.repeat);}
  else if(e.key==='Escape')deactivate();
});
document.addEventListener('click',e=>{
  const sel=getSelection();if(sel&&!sel.isCollapsed)return;
  if(!units.length||e.target.closest('a,button,nav'))return;
  const u=e.target.closest('.rd');if(!u)return;
  const i=units.indexOf(u);if(i<0)return;
  if(on){set(i,false);}else activate(i);
});

function init(){
  if(ready)return;ready=true;
  for(const el of Array.from(document.querySelectorAll(ALL))){
    if(el.tagName==='MJX-CONTAINER'){if(!el.parentElement.closest(BLK)){el.classList.add('rd');units.push(el);}continue;}
    if(el.querySelector(BLK))continue;
    units.push(...build(el));
  }
  hint();
}
addEventListener('load',()=>{
  const p=window.MathJax&&window.MathJax.startup&&window.MathJax.startup.promise;
  Promise.resolve(p).then(init,init);
});
})();
</script>
</body>
</html>

<main class="min-w-0 flex-1">
<header>
<p class="ui text-sm" style="color:var(--mute)">arXiv cs.AI preprint (workshop: AI for Chip Design) &middot; arXiv:2610.06790v1, 5 Oct 2026 &middot; read: full text and appendix; figure images not seen</p>
<h1 class="ui text-3xl font-semibold leading-tight mt-1">Back to the Future: Rethinking EDA Infrastructure for Agentic Systems in Chip Design Verification</h1>
<p class="ui text-sm mt-1" style="color:var(--mute)">Je Yang (Columbia University; work done while interning at Google), Ivan Lobov (Google DeepMind), Thomas Karpati (Google)</p>
<p class="mt-4"><b>Verdict.</b> A sensible, cheap pattern: load the VCD into a two-table SQLite store, prune clocks and silent nets, and let tool-calling agents query it. The evidence is thin: a self-built 150-query benchmark scored 143/150 with no external baseline, no accuracy-under-pruning run, and a thesis (infrastructure, not model capability, is the bottleneck) that is never tested without the database. Reuse the idea and treat the numbers as illustrative; the weakest points are the undefined "coverage" metric and root-cause analysis that is advertised but never evaluated. <span class="conf">overall confidence 70%</span></p>
</header>

<h2 class="s" id="mot">Motivation<span class="conf">86%</span></h2>
<p>The authors argue that LLM-for-EDA work is lopsided: approximately 74.6% of studies target static RTL generation, so post-simulation waveform debugging is neglected, even though verification is said to take over 70% of the design cycle. Engineers debug by scrubbing waveform viewers, while agents are locked out because raw ASCII dumps exceed context windows by orders of magnitude, carry no semantic index and expose no query interface. The paper names this gap "Infrastructural Impedance".</p>
<p><b>Convincing?</b> The pain point is real and the diagnosis (agents see logs, humans see waveforms) is persuasive. The two supporting statistics are weaker than they look: 74.6% comes from a 2024 scoping review that cannot reflect the 2025-26 agentic wave, and the 70% figure is cited to a Cadence web page.</p>

<h2 class="s" id="core">Core idea<span class="conf">88%</span></h2>
<p>Two coupled parts. Offline, a VCD dump is loaded into SQLite as <code>signal_metadata</code> (id, RTL signal name, variable type) and <code>signal_changes</code> (timestamp, value, foreign key to metadata), with rows pruned at insertion. Online, an orchestrator and three agents turn natural-language questions into tool or SQL calls over that database.</p>
<p>The pruning policies, as I formalize the paper's prose (it gives no equation): let \(n_s\) be the transition count of signal \(s\), \(S\) the set of signals and \(\mathcal{C}\subseteq S\) the clocks.</p>
\[ \mathrm{keep}(s)\iff s\notin\mathcal{C}\ \wedge\ n_s\ge\tau,\qquad \tau_{\text{zero}}=1,\quad \tau_{\text{avg}}=\bar n=\frac{1}{|S|}\sum_{s\in S}n_s \]
<p>Table 1 appears to apply the policies cumulatively. Splitting its reductions into marginal contributions shows that clock removal does nearly all the work (percentage points of the unpruned 1,420 MB):</p>
\[ 86.97\%\;\approx\;\underbrace{66.2}_{\text{clocks}}+\underbrace{6.0}_{\text{zero-toggle}}+\underbrace{14.8}_{\text{below-average}}\ \text{pp} \]
<figure data-n="1" data-a="S3.F1"><figcaption>Fig. 1. BTTF overview: offline waveform preprocessing into waves.db, online agentic orchestration over it.</figcaption></figure>
<ul class="b">
<li><b>Orchestration Agent:</b> decomposes the query into sub-goals, routes them and audits the merged answer.</li>
<li><b>Analysis Agent:</b> composes queries for scope discovery, event inspection and toggle counting, with an iterative function-calling loop for hotspots.</li>
<li><b>Verification Agent:</b> ties waveform anomalies to versioned source by matching five SVA macro names (ASSERT, ASSERT_STATIC, ASSERT_NEVER, ASSERT_KNOWN, COVER) with regular expressions.</li>
<li><b>Evaluation Agent:</b> audits candidate queries on correctness, completeness, efficiency, schema adherence and context understanding.</li>
</ul>
<p>Agents communicate only through a shared session context, with a self-correction loop on failed queries. <b>My read:</b> the pruning pipeline is the only part with a measured trade-off. The agent layer gets equal billing in the paper, so I treat it as a second core idea even though its evidence is thin.</p>

<h2 class="s" id="diff">Distinguishing factor<span class="conf">62%</span></h2>
<ul class="b">
<li><b>LLM RTL-generation and verification agents</b> (VeriMoA, MAGE, UVLLM, FVEval). The paper says these work on static source, assertion lists or small simulation logs, and BTTF differs by consuming simulation waveforms. I did not open these papers to check that characterization.</li>
<li><b>Waveform-aware coding agents</b> (VerilogCoder, AST-based waveform tracing). The closest prior work, dismissed as tied to small code-centric examples. There is no head-to-head run, so the difference in scale is asserted, not shown.</li>
<li><b>Text-to-SQL</b> (Gao et al. 2023). Used as an enabling capability, not extended. With two tables the SQL itself is easy; the hard parts are scope-name resolution and timescales, which the paper handles with helper tools.</li>
<li><b>Not discussed:</b> conventional waveform tooling such as viewer scripting, vendor waveform-database APIs and Python VCD parsers (from my background knowledge, unverified). A VCD already consists of declarations plus timestamped changes, which is exactly the two tables, so the schema is a transcription. The contribution is the pruning policies and the agent tool layer.</li>
</ul>
<p>No earlier version by the same authors is cited, so there is no comparison to a prior BTTF.</p>

<h2 class="s" id="eval">Evaluation, conclusions, rigor<span class="conf">72%</span></h2>
<div class="wrap"><table>
<thead><tr><th>Section</th><th>Setup</th><th>Reported result</th><th>My read</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td>§4, Table 1</td><td>One benchmark database (4.2 GB of VCD, 18,450 nets), four pruning policies.</td><td>Footprint 1,420 MB to 480 / 395 / 185 MB (66.20 / 72.18 / 86.97% smaller). Latency 410.5 to 125.2 / 94.8 / 41.6 ms. "Coverage" 100 / 98.51 / 100 / 64.23%.</td><td>Arithmetic checks out. "Coverage" is never defined; 98.51% after removing rows that make up two thirds of the table is only plausible as a net count. No accuracy-versus-policy run, which is the experiment pruning needs. Latency falls at least in proportion to size, which suggests scans rather than an index on (signal, time).</td></tr>
<tr><td>§4, Fig. 2</td><td>Database generation with batched insertion.</td><td>240.5 s to 83.2 s, a \(2.89\times\) speed-up.</td><td>Standard executemany batching; gains flatten between the last two batch sizes (85.4 s to 83.2 s). The claim that it removes the bottleneck for multi-terabyte waveforms rests on a 4.2 GB test.</td></tr>
<tr><td>§4, Table 2</td><td>150 expert-annotated queries, 9 task types, Gemini-2.5-Pro, no baseline.</td><td>95.33% overall; 84.62% (static SVA) to 100% per type.</td><td>Equals \(143/150\), Wilson 95% interval about 90.7 to 97.8%. The weaker types rest on roughly 8 to 13 queries each (implied by 87.50, 91.67, 84.62 and 90.91%), so one query moves a type by 8 to 13 points. The scoring rule for "execution accuracy" is not stated, and five of nine types are single-hop lookups.</td></tr>
<tr><td>§4, agent ablation</td><td>Monolithic agent versus BTTF, autorater using the Evaluation Agent's five criteria.</td><td>Monolithic 3.35/5; BTTF 17.3% higher (about 3.93/5, my arithmetic); \(1.96\times\) latency.</td><td>Judge model unnamed, and the rubric is the one the Evaluation Agent itself applies, which is circular. No execution accuracy for the single agent, no seeds, no variance.</td></tr>
<tr><td>App. A, Table 3</td><td>Worked examples of tool calls and outputs.</td><td>One example per task type.</td><td>Two entries disagree with themselves: the hotspot example is described as sliding 100 ns intervals (Table 2 asks for a 100 ns window) yet reports a 2,332 ns range with 27 toggles, and a toggle count called with picosecond arguments 6,800,000 to 8,000,000 reports the interval 680 to 800 ns.</td></tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p><b>Paper's conclusion.</b> The binding constraint on agentic waveform debugging is data infrastructure, not model capability, and agent specialization adds a 17.3% quality gain. <b>My read:</b> not supported. There is no run without the database (raw VCD plus a code interpreter, say), only one model is used, and the closing paragraph defers multi-hop causal tracing to larger models, which concedes that capability still binds. The text also calls the system hallucination-free while 7 of 150 queries fail.</p>

<h2 class="s" id="icymi">In case you missed it<span class="conf">70%</span></h2>
<div class="item"><span class="chip neg">−</span><b>Root-cause analysis is advertised, not evaluated.</b> Table 2 has no root-cause category, and the "backward causal tracing" of Appendix A.1 looks up the prior values of the assertion's own operands, which names the violating symptom rather than a cause upstream in the design. The intro's "formal verification" is dynamic assertion checking on a single trace.</div>
<div class="item"><span class="chip neg">−</span><b>Less open-ended than "text-to-SQL" suggests.</b> The Verification Agent is five hard-coded macro names matched by regex plus per-rule checkers such as <code>verify_known_rule</code>, and most Analysis Agent actions in Table 3 are named helper functions. This is closer to natural-language tool selection.</div>
<div class="item"><span class="chip neg">−</span><b>Pruning blind spots.</b> Zero-toggle pruning drops constant values, yet a signal that never moves is a common bug symptom. Clock pruning is justified by timestamps preserving temporal anchoring, but the paper never says the clock period is stored, while the ASSERT_KNOWN example samples at clock edges and the A.1 example reports a net going unknown "while clk was high".</div>
<div class="item"><span class="chip neg">−</span><b>Scale gap.</b> The motivation talks about terabytes and dumps above 10 GB; the benchmark is 4.2 GB, 18,450 nets and 50 to 800 ns windows, and the unpruned database (1,420 MB) is already smaller than the VCD. No code or benchmark release is mentioned.</div>
<div class="item"><span class="chip neu">·</span><b>"On-the-fly" is doing a lot of work.</b> Pruning by the design-wide average toggle rate needs global statistics, so it cannot be single pass, the ingestion cost of that policy is not reported, and streaming ingestion is listed as future work.</div>
<div class="item"><span class="chip neu">·</span><b>Loose citations.</b> A prefix-caching systems paper (KVflow) and a Copilot robustness study are cited as EDA-verification evidence, in a reference list of only 12 entries.</div>
<div class="item"><span class="chip pos">+</span><b>Right kind of boring.</b> Windowed and aggregate questions (toggle counts, first time a value occurs) are exactly what a context window cannot hold and a database answers trivially, and Table 3 gives concrete calls that make the design easy to reimplement.</div>
<div class="item"><span class="chip pos">+</span><b>An explicit lossy boundary.</b> The pruning ladder with a stated 35.77% coverage loss is the most reusable contribution, once the fidelity metric is defined.</div>

<h2 class="s" id="judge">Cautious judgement<span class="conf">66%</span></h2>
<div class="wrap"><table>
<thead><tr><th>Question</th><th>Answer</th><th>Conf.</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td>Fair comparison to external baselines?</td><td>No external baseline at all. Missing: VerilogCoder's waveform tracer, a code-interpreter agent over a raw or windowed VCD, and vendor scripting. The only comparison is internal and judged on the system's own rubric.</td><td>88%</td></tr>
<tr><td>Invents its own FoM?</td><td>Yes, three: "coverage" (undefined, Table 1), "execution accuracy" (scoring unstated) and the five-criterion autorater score, whose 17.3% is a relative change on a custom 5-point scale and cannot be converted to a standard metric.</td><td>80%</td></tr>
<tr><td>Rewrite of an existing idea?</td><td>Largely. The VCD's own declarations-plus-changes layout is the two-table schema, and text-to-SQL (Gao et al. 2023) and role-split agent stacks (VeriMoA, MAGE) are existing patterns. New: the pruning policies and the SVA-macro checker.</td><td>70%</td></tr>
<tr><td>Reliable enough to reuse?</td><td>The pattern yes, the numbers no. No code or benchmark, the effect of pruning on answer accuracy is unmeasured, and Table 3 has unit and window inconsistencies.</td><td>62%</td></tr>
</tbody></table></div>

<p>Your MD was attached from the start, so my read is not blind to it; I wrote my notes from the paper text first and then re-opened the MD. Mine is the digest above; yours is quoted or paraphrased here, with the differences in the last column.</p>

const BASE='https://arxiv.org/html/2610.06790v1/';
const MAP={1:'overall.png'};
document.querySelectorAll('figure[data-n]').forEach(f=>{
  const n=f.dataset.n,img=new Image();img.loading='lazy';img.alt='Figure '+n;
  img.onerror=()=>{const a=document.createElement('a');a.href='https://arxiv.org/html/2610.06790#'+f.dataset.a;a.textContent='Open Fig. '+n+' on arXiv';a.className='ui text-sm underline';a.style.color='var(--teal)';img.replaceWith(a);};

<script>
/* Reading marker. R or a click on a sentence starts it; Up/Down (or k/j) move it; Esc or R stops it.
   Arrow keys scroll the page normally unless the marker is active. Runs after MathJax has typeset. */
(function(){
const BLK='main p:not(.cite),main li:not(.cite),main .item,main blockquote,main figcaption,main td';
const ALL=BLK+',main mjx-container[display="true"]';
const ABBR=/(?:\b(?:e\.g|i\.e|et al|etc|vs|cf|Figs?|Eqs?|Secs?|Tab|Refs?|Algs?|approx|resp|No)|\b[A-Z])\.$/;
const OBJ='\uFFFC';
let units=[],idx=-1,on=false,ready=false,hintEl;

function tokens(block){
  const items=[];let run=[];
  const flush=()=>{if(run.length)items.push({run});run=[];};
  const walk=n=>{
    for(let c=n.firstChild;c;c=c.nextSibling){
      if(c.nodeType===3){run.push({t:c});}
      else if(c.nodeType===1){
        const tag=c.tagName;
        if(tag==='MJX-CONTAINER'){
          if(c.getAttribute('display')==='true'){flush();items.push({disp:c});}
          else run.push({a:c});
        }else if(tag==='SCRIPT'||tag==='STYLE'||tag==='MJX-ASSISTIVE-MML'||tag==='IMG'){}
        else walk(c);
      }
    }
  };
  walk(block);flush();return items;
}

function sentences(run){
  let s='';const map=[];
  for(const p of run){
    if(p.t){const v=p.t.nodeValue;for(let i=0;i<v.length;i++)map.push({n:p.t,o:i,L:v.length});s+=v;}
    else{map.push({a:p.a});s+=OBJ;}
  }
  const cuts=[0];
  const re=/[.!?]+["'’”)\]]*(?=\s+[A-Z0-9"'“‘(\[\uFFFC])/g;
  let m;
  while((m=re.exec(s))){const end=m.index+m[0].length;if(!ABBR.test(s.slice(Math.max(0,end-10),end)))cuts.push(end);}
  cuts.push(s.length);
  const out=[];
  for(let i=0;i<cuts.length-1;i++){
    let a=cuts[i],b=cuts[i+1];
    while(a<b&&/\s/.test(s[a]))a++;
    while(b>a&&/\s/.test(s[b-1]))b--;
    if(b>a)out.push([a,b]);
  }
  return {out,map};
}

function rangeFor(block,map,a,b){
  const r=document.createRange(),f=map[a],l=map[b-1];
  if(f.a)r.setStartBefore(f.a);
  else if(f.o===0){let n=f.n;while(n.parentNode!==block&&n===n.parentNode.firstChild)n=n.parentNode;r.setStartBefore(n);}
  else r.setStart(f.n,f.o);
  if(l.a)r.setEndAfter(l.a);
  else if(l.o===l.L-1){let n=l.n;while(n.parentNode!==block&&n===n.parentNode.lastChild)n=n.parentNode;r.setEndAfter(n);}
  else r.setEnd(l.n,l.o+1);
  return r;
}

function build(block){
  const out=[];
  for(const it of tokens(block)){
    if(it.disp){it.disp.classList.add('rd');out.push(it.disp);continue;}
    const {out:ss,map}=sentences(it.run),spans=[];
    for(let i=ss.length-1;i>=0;i--){   /* last sentence first, so earlier offsets stay valid */
      const r=rangeFor(block,map,ss[i][0],ss[i][1]);
      const sp=document.createElement('span');sp.className='rd';
      sp.appendChild(r.extractContents());r.insertNode(sp);spans.push(sp);
    }
    out.push(...spans.reverse());
  }
  return out;
}

function hint(){
  if(!hintEl){hintEl=document.createElement('div');hintEl.id='rd-hint';document.body.appendChild(hintEl);}
  hintEl.textContent=on?'\u2191 \u2193 move \u00b7 Esc exit':'R: reading marker';
}
function inView(i){const r=units[i].getBoundingClientRect();return r.bottom>0&&r.top<innerHeight;}
function firstVisible(){for(let i=0;i<units.length;i++)if(units[i].getBoundingClientRect().top>=48)return i;return units.length-1;}
function ensure(u,fast){
  const r=u.getBoundingClientRect(),h=innerHeight;
  if(r.top<h*.18||r.bottom>h*.78)scrollBy({top:r.top-h*.38,behavior:fast?'auto':'smooth'});
}
function set(i,scroll,fast){
  if(units[idx])units[idx].classList.remove('on');
  idx=Math.max(0,Math.min(units.length-1,i));
  units[idx].classList.add('on');
  if(scroll)ensure(units[idx],fast);
}
function activate(i){
  on=true;document.body.classList.add('rd-active');
  set(i!==undefined?i:(idx>=0&&inView(idx)?idx:firstVisible()),i===undefined);hint();
}
function deactivate(){
  on=false;document.body.classList.remove('rd-active');
  if(units[idx])units[idx].classList.remove('on');hint();
}

document.addEventListener('keydown',e=>{
  if(!units.length||e.ctrlKey||e.metaKey||e.altKey)return;
  const t=e.target;if(t&&(/^(INPUT|TEXTAREA|SELECT)$/.test(t.tagName)||t.isContentEditable))return;
  if(e.key==='r'||e.key==='R'){e.preventDefault();on?deactivate():activate();return;}
  if(!on)return;
  if(e.key==='ArrowDown'||e.key==='j'){e.preventDefault();set(idx+1,true,e.repeat);}
  else if(e.key==='ArrowUp'||e.key==='k'){e.preventDefault();set(idx-1,true,e.repeat);}
  else if(e.key==='Escape')deactivate();
});
document.addEventListener('click',e=>{
  const sel=getSelection();if(sel&&!sel.isCollapsed)return;
  if(!units.length||e.target.closest('a,button,nav'))return;
  const u=e.target.closest('.rd');if(!u)return;
  const i=units.indexOf(u);if(i<0)return;
  if(on){set(i,false);}else activate(i);
});

function init(){
  if(ready)return;ready=true;
  for(const el of Array.from(document.querySelectorAll(ALL))){
    if(el.tagName==='MJX-CONTAINER'){if(!el.parentElement.closest(BLK)){el.classList.add('rd');units.push(el);}continue;}
    if(el.querySelector(BLK))continue;
    units.push(...build(el));
  }
  hint();
}
addEventListener('load',()=>{
  const p=window.MathJax&&window.MathJax.startup&&window.MathJax.startup.promise;
  Promise.resolve(p).then(init,init);
});
})();
</script>
</body>
</html>

=== src/cli.js ===

#!/usr/bin/env node
/**
 * paper-digest CLI.
 *
 * Subcommands:
 *
 *   paper-digest prompt <source> [--out <file>]
 *       Fetch the source and print the master prompt (Markdown-output instructions).
 *       Use this to hand the prompt to a chat AI, which returns the digest Markdown.
 *
 *   paper-digest digest <source> [--out <dir>]
 *       Full pipeline: fetch -> OpenRouter agent (Markdown) -> template -> HTML.
 *
 *   paper-digest template <digest.md> [--out <dir>] [--stdout]
 *       Take an already-written digest Markdown (with YAML frontmatter) and render
 *       it into the HTML template. Reads Markdown from a file argument, from `-`,
 *       or from stdin (piped). With --stdout the rendered HTML is written to stdout
 *       instead of a file.
 *
 * source: an arXiv id/link, a URL, raw HTML text, or a local PDF path.
 */

import { resolve } from "./fetch.js";
import { buildMasterPrompt, extractFinal, extractDraft } from "./prompt.js";
import { digestSource, loadConfig, createClient } from "./digest.js";
import { renderString, renderFile, outputFilenameBase, sanitize, shortenedTitle, extractYear, venueAbbv } from "./template.js";
import { extractMarkdown } from "./llm.js";
import { readFile, mkdir, writeFile } from "fs/promises";
import { join } from "path";
import { existsSync, readFileSync } from "fs";
import { parseHTML, Node } from "linkedom";

const DEFAULT_OUT = "outputs";

function parseArgs(argv) {
  const args = argv.slice(2);
  const subcommand = args[0];
  const rest = args.slice(1);
  let out = DEFAULT_OUT;
  let stdout = false;
  const positional = [];
  for (let i = 0; i < rest.length; i++) {
    if (rest[i] === "--out" || rest[i] === "-o") {
      out = rest[++i];
    } else if (rest[i] === "--stdout" || rest[i] === "-s") {
      stdout = true;
    } else {
      positional.push(rest[i]);
    }
  }
  return { subcommand, out, stdout, positional };
}

async function readStdin() {
  const chunks = [];
  for await (const chunk of process.stdin) {
    chunks.push(chunk);
  }
  return Buffer.concat(chunks).toString("utf8");
}

function readLocalIfFile(source) {
  if (existsSync(source)) {
    return readFileSync(source, "utf8");
  }
  return source;
}

async function runPrompt(source) {
  const resolved = await resolve(readLocalIfFile(source));
  resolved.whatRead =
    resolved.kind === "arxiv" && !resolved.html
      ? "arXiv abstract page only (no HTML version)"
      : resolved.kind === "arxiv"
        ? "arXiv HTML full text"
        : "provided HTML";
  const prompt = buildMasterPrompt({
    paperText: resolved.html ? extractTextFromHtml(resolved.html) : "",
    figures: resolved.figures || [],
    baseUrl: resolved.baseUrl || "",
    whatRead: resolved.whatRead,
  });
  return { prompt, source: resolved };
}

export async function runDigest(source, { outDir, cfg, client } = {}) {
  const resolved = await resolve(readLocalIfFile(source));
  if (resolved.kind === "pdf") {
    throw new Error("PDF text extraction is not implemented; supply HTML or a URL.");
  }
  const paperText = resolved.html ? extractTextFromHtml(resolved.html) : "";
  resolved.whatRead =
    resolved.kind === "arxiv" && !resolved.html
      ? "arXiv abstract page only (no HTML version)"
      : resolved.kind === "arxiv"
        ? "arXiv HTML full text"
        : "provided HTML";

  const llm = client || createClient(cfg);
  const md = await digestSource(resolved, { client: llm });

  // md.raw is the full raw agent response (all blocks); unwrap the clean
  // document (<final> else <draft>) to persist as the digest Markdown.
  let mdText = extractFinal(md.raw) ?? extractDraft(md.raw) ?? md.raw;

  // Inject figures and baseUrl into frontmatter so standalone template
  // rendering can resolve image URLs (figures are parsed at fetch time).
  const fmEnd = mdText.indexOf("\n---\n", 4);
  if (fmEnd !== -1) {
    const injection = `figures: ${JSON.stringify(md.figures || [])}\nbaseUrl: ${JSON.stringify(md.baseUrl || "")}\n`;
    mdText = mdText.slice(0, fmEnd + 1) + injection + mdText.slice(fmEnd + 1);
  }

  // Persist intermediate digest Markdown next to the output.
  await mkdir(outDir, { recursive: true });
  const baseName = outputFilenameBase(md, resolved);
  const mdPath = join(outDir, baseName + ".md");
  await writeFile(mdPath, mdText, "utf8");

  // Persist the full raw agent response (all blocks) beside the digest so
  // failures can be diagnosed without re-running the model.
  await writeFile(join(outDir, baseName + ".response.txt"), md.raw, "utf8");

  const html = await renderString(md, resolved);
  const htmlPath = join(outDir, baseName + ".html");
  await writeFile(htmlPath, html, "utf8");

  return { digest: md, mdPath, htmlPath, source: resolved };
}

async function runTemplate(mdPath, { outDir, stdout }) {
  let text;
  let sourceLabel;
  if (mdPath === undefined || mdPath === "-" || mdPath === "") {
    text = await readStdin();
    sourceLabel = "<stdin>";
  } else {
    text = await readFile(mdPath, "utf8");
    sourceLabel = mdPath;
  }
  // Unwrap an agent three-block response (<draft><check><final>) so the
  // Markdown document inside <final> is what gets templated. Takes the last
  // <final> match (like extractFinal in prompt.js), falls back to <draft>,
  // and falls back to the raw text if neither wrapper is present.
  let unwrapped = text;
  const finalMatches = [...String(text).matchAll(/<final>([\s\S]*?)<\/final>/g)];
  if (finalMatches.length > 0) {
    unwrapped = finalMatches[finalMatches.length - 1][1].trim();
  } else {
    const draftMatches = [...String(text).matchAll(/<draft>([\s\S]*?)<\/draft>/g)];
    if (draftMatches.length > 0) {
      unwrapped = draftMatches[draftMatches.length - 1][1].trim();
    }
  }
  const salvagedByUnwrap = unwrapped !== text;
  const { metadata, body, _salvaged, raw } = extractMarkdown(unwrapped);
  if (_salvaged || salvagedByUnwrap) {
    console.warn(`[paper-digest] salvaged malformed Markdown from ${sourceLabel}`);
  }
  // Reconstruct a minimal source descriptor from whatever metadata the markdown carries.
  const source = {
    figures: metadata.figures || [],
    baseUrl: metadata.baseUrl || "",
    value: sourceLabel,
  };
  const digest = {
    ...metadata,
    sections: [], 
    body,
    raw,
  };
  // We need to parse sections from body. 
  const { splitSections, parseIcymi, parseJudge } = await import("./digest.js");
  const sections = splitSections(body);
  for (const s of sections) {
    if (s.id === "icymi") s.entries = parseIcymi(s.content);
    if (s.id === "judge") s.rows = parseJudge(s.content);
  }
  digest.sections = sections;
  const html = await renderString(digest, source);
  let outPath = null;
  if (!stdout) {
    outPath = await renderFile(digest, source, outDir);
  }
  return { html, digest, outPath };
}

const HEADING_TAGS = new Set(["h1", "h2", "h3", "h4", "h5", "h6"]);

/**
 * Extract the paper's body text from HTML for the agent's source text.
 *
 * Chrome is dropped by scoping to the article body (LaTeXML:
 * article.ltx_document or .ltx_page_content; anything else falls back to
 * <body>), not by blacklisting strings. Each <math> contributes its
 * alttext once as inline math; headings get a markdown heading marker;
 * tables are serialised as caption plus pipe-separated rows. Figures are
 * not extracted here — they are carried through the figure list.
 */
// Block-level elements: their children are separated by blank lines.
// Inline elements: their children are joined with spaces.
const BLOCK_TAGS = new Set([
  "p",
  "div",
  "section",
  "article",
  "aside",
  "header",
  "footer",
  "nav",
  "main",
  "body",
  "html",
  "h1",
  "h2",
  "h3",
  "h4",
  "h5",
  "h6",
  "blockquote",
  "pre",
  "table",
  "caption",
  "tr",
  "td",
  "th",
  "li",
  "dd",
  "dt",
  "figcaption",
]);

// Elements that carry no extractable text for the source dump.
const DROP_TAGS = new Set(["figure", "script", "style", "img", "object", "svg"]);

export function extractTextFromHtml(html) {
  const { document } = parseHTML(html);

  const textOf = (el) => {
    let t = "";
    const collect = (node) => {
      if (node.nodeType === Node.TEXT_NODE) {
        t += node.textContent;
      } else if (node.nodeType === Node.ELEMENT_NODE) {
        if (node.tagName.toLowerCase() === "math") {
          const alt = node.getAttribute("alttext");
          if (alt) t += alt;
          return;
        }
        for (const c of node.childNodes) collect(c);
      }
    };
    collect(el);
    return t.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
  };

  const walk = (node) => {
    switch (node.nodeType) {
      case Node.TEXT_NODE:
        return node.textContent.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
      case Node.DOCUMENT_NODE:
      case Node.DOCUMENT_FRAGMENT_NODE:
        return [...node.childNodes]
          .map(walk)
          .filter(Boolean)
          .join("\n\n");
      case Node.ELEMENT_NODE: {
        const tag = node.tagName.toLowerCase();

        // Math: emit the alttext once as inline math; drop the rendered form.
        if (tag === "math") {
          const alt = node.getAttribute("alttext");
          return alt ? `\\(${alt}\\)` : "";
        }
        // Figures are carried through the figure list, not the source text.
        if (DROP_TAGS.has(tag)) return "";

        if (HEADING_TAGS.has(tag)) {
          return (
            "#".repeat(Math.min(Number(tag[1]) || 1, 2)) + " " + textOf(node)
          );
        }

        if (tag === "table") {
          const parts = [];
          const caption = node.querySelector("caption");
          if (caption) {
            const cap = textOf(caption);
            if (cap) parts.push(cap);
          }
          for (const tr of node.querySelectorAll("tr")) {
            const cells = [...tr.querySelectorAll("th, td")].map(textOf);
            if (cells.length) parts.push("| " + cells.join(" | ") + " |");
          }
          return parts.join("\n");
        }

        const kids = [...node.childNodes]
          .map((c) => [c, walk(c)])
          .filter(([, t]) => t);
        const parts = [];
        let pending = null;
        const flush = () => {
          if (pending) {
            parts.push(pending);
            pending = null;
          }
        };
        for (const [c, t] of kids) {
          const isBlock = c.nodeType === Node.ELEMENT_NODE && BLOCK_TAGS.has(c.tagName.toLowerCase());
          if (isBlock) {
            flush();
            parts.push(t);
          } else {
            pending = pending ? pending + " " + t : t;
          }
        }
        flush();
        return BLOCK_TAGS.has(tag) ? parts.join("\n\n") : parts.join(" ");
      }
      default:
        return "";
    }
  };

  // Scope to the paper body (LaTeXML: article.ltx_document or
  // .ltx_page_content); fall back to <body>, then the document element.
  // Pick the first candidate that yields actual text so fragment inputs
  // (e.g. a bare <p>) still produce output.
  const candidates = [
    document.querySelector("article.ltx_document, .ltx_page_content"),
    document.body,
    document.documentElement,
    document,
  ];
  let result = "";
  for (const c of candidates) {
    if (!c) continue;
    result = walk(c).trim();
    if (result) break;
  }
  return result;
}

export async function run(argv) {
  const { subcommand, out, stdout, positional } = parseArgs(argv);

  if (subcommand === "prompt") {
    if (positional.length === 0) {
      console.error("Usage: paper-digest prompt <source> [--out <file>]");
      process.exit(1);
    }
    const { prompt, source } = await runPrompt(positional[0]);
    if (out !== DEFAULT_OUT) {
      await writeFile(out, prompt, "utf8");
      console.log(`Wrote prompt to ${out}`);
    } else {
      process.stdout.write(prompt);
    }
    return;
  }

  if (subcommand === "digest") {
    if (positional.length === 0) {
      console.error("Usage: paper-digest digest <source> [--out <dir>]");
      process.exit(1);
    }
    const cfg = loadConfig();
    const result = await runDigest(positional[0], { outDir: out, cfg });
    console.log(`Wrote ${result.htmlPath}`);
    console.log(`Digest Markdown: ${result.mdPath}`);
    console.log(`Verdict: ${result.digest.verdict}`);
    return;
  }

  if (subcommand === "template") {
    if (positional.length > 1) {
      console.error("Usage: paper-digest template [digest.md|-] [--out <dir>] [--stdout]");
      process.exit(1);
    }
    if (positional.length === 0 && process.stdin.isTTY) {
      console.error("Usage: paper-digest template [digest.md|-] [--out <dir>] [--stdout]");
      process.exit(1);
    }
    const { html, digest, outPath } = await runTemplate(positional[0], { outDir: out, stdout });
    if (stdout) {
      process.stdout.write(html);
      return;
    }
    console.log(`${outPath}`); // pipable
    return;
  }

  console.error(
    "Usage: paper-digest <prompt|digest|template> <source|digest.md> [--out <dir|file>] [--stdout]"
  );
  process.exit(1);
}

const mainFile = process.argv[1] || "";
if (mainFile && (mainFile.endsWith("src/cli.js") || mainFile.endsWith("src\\cli.js"))) {
  run(process.argv).catch((err) => {
    console.error(err);
    process.exit(1);
  });
}

=== src/digest.js ===

/**
 * Agent orchestration: build the master prompt, call the OpenRouter LLM,
 * parse the Markdown response into a digest object.
 *
 * The LLM client (src/llm.js) follows the reference Python `LLM` class:
 * OpenRouter API key from OPENROUTER_API_KEY or ~/.paper_digest_key,
 * models tried in order with retries and a per-run cap.
 */

import { buildMasterPrompt, extractFinal, extractDraft } from "./prompt.js";
import { LLM, loadConfig, extractMarkdown } from "./llm.js";

const SYSTEM = "You are a careful, skeptical research-paper reader. Output only valid Markdown with YAML frontmatter.";

const SECTION_IDS = {
  "motivation": "mot",
  "core idea": "core",
  "distinguishing factor": "diff",
  "evaluation and conclusions": "eval",
  "in case you missed it": "icymi",
  "cautious judgement": "judge",
  "sources and limits": "src",
};

export { splitSections, parseIcymi, parseJudge, loadConfig };

/**
 * Build a pre-configured OpenRouter LLM client.
 *
 * @param {object} [cfg] - LLM config (models, temperature, max_tokens, ...)
 * @returns {LLM}
 */
export function createClient(cfg) {
  return new LLM(cfg);
}

/**
 * Split a markdown body into sections keyed by `## ` headers.
 *
 * @param {string} body - markdown body text
 * @returns {Array<{id:string, title:string, content:string, raw:string}>}
 */
function splitSections(body) {
  const lines = body.split("\n");
  const sections = [];
  let current = null;
  let buf = [];

  for (const line of lines) {
    const h = /^##\s+(.+)$/.exec(line);
    if (h) {
      if (current) {
        current.content = buf.join("\n").trim();
        sections.push(current);
      }
      const rawTitle = h[1].trim();
      // A section header may carry an inline confidence, e.g.
      // "## Motivation (Confidence: 90%)". Strip it so the clean title
      // matches the known section ids, and carry the confidence through.
      const confM = /\(\s*[Cc]onfidence:\s*(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)\s*%?\s*\)\s*$/i.exec(rawTitle);
      let title = rawTitle;
      let confidence = null;
      if (confM) {
        confidence = Number(confM[1]);
        title = rawTitle.slice(0, confM.index).trimEnd();
      }
      const id = SECTION_IDS[title.toLowerCase()] || slug(title);
      current = { id, title, content: "", raw: "", confidence };
      buf = [];
    } else {
      if (current) buf.push(line);
    }
  }
  if (current) {
    current.content = buf.join("\n").trim();
    sections.push(current);
  }
  return sections;
}

function slug(title) {
  return String(title)
    .toLowerCase()
    .replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, "-")
    .replace(/(^-|-$)/g, "")
    .slice(0, 20);
}

/**
 * Parse icymi tag list entries from markdown.
 *
 * Entries look like: - [pos] text
 * @param {string} content - markdown content of the icymi section
 * @returns {Array<{text:string, tag:string}>}
 */
function parseIcymi(content) {
  const entries = [];
  // Entries look like: "- [pos] text" or "* `[neu]` text" (backticks around
  // the tag are optional).
  const re = /^[*-]\s*`?\[(pos|neg|neu)\]`?\s*(.*)$/;
  for (const line of content.split("\n")) {
    const m = re.exec(line);
    if (m) {
      entries.push({ text: m[2].trim(), tag: m[1] });
    }
  }
  return entries;
}

/**
 * Parse judge table rows from markdown.
 *
 * Rows are markdown table rows: | Question | Answer | Confidence |
 * @param {string} content - markdown content of the judge section
 * @returns {Array<{question:string, answer:string, conf:number}>}
 */
function parseJudge(content) {
  const rows = [];
  const lines = content.split("\n");
  for (const line of lines) {
    const m = /^\s*\|\s*([^|]*?)\s*\|\s*([^|]*?)\s*\|\s*([^|]*?)\s*\|?\s*$/.exec(line);
    if (!m) continue;
    const q = m[1].trim();
    const a = m[2].trim();
    const c = m[3].trim();
    // Skip header and separator rows
    if (q.toLowerCase() === "question") continue;
    if (/^[-:\s|]+$/.test(q)) continue;
    const conf = Number(c.replace("%", "").trim()) || 0;
    rows.push({ question: q, answer: a, conf });
  }
  return rows;
}

/**
 * Run the agent on a resolved source. Returns the parsed digest object.
 *
 * @param {object} source - output of fetch.resolve()
 * @param {object} [opts]
 * @param {LLM} [opts.client] - pre-built LLM client
 * @param {object} [opts.cfg] - LLM config (passed to LLM constructor)
 * @returns {Promise<object>}
 */
export async function digestSource(source, { client, cfg } = {}) {
  const llm = client || createClient(cfg);
  const user = buildMasterPrompt({
    paperText: source.paperText || "",
    figures: source.figures || [],
    baseUrl: source.baseUrl || "",
    whatRead: source.whatRead || "unknown",
  });
  const response = await llm.chat(SYSTEM, user);
  let finalMd = extractFinal(response);
  if (finalMd === null) {
    // The check asked for fixes but no <final> was emitted: warn and render
    // the draft unchanged. Never block or re-prompt (single request rule).
    const fixM = /Fixes to apply:\s*(\d+)/.exec(response);
    if (fixM && Number(fixM[1]) > 0) {
      console.warn(
        `[paper-digest] Fixes to apply: ${fixM[1]} reported in <check> but no <final> block was returned; rendering the draft unchanged (single request rule: no re-prompt)`,
      );
    }
    // A clean check ("Fixes to apply: 0") means the agent correctly omitted
    // <final>; the draft IS the document. Fall back to it before failing.
    finalMd = extractDraft(response);
    if (finalMd === null) {
      throw new Error(
        "agent response contains no <final> or <draft> block (the response may have been truncated)",
      );
    }
  }
  const parsed = extractMarkdown(finalMd);

  const sections = splitSections(parsed.body);

  // Attach section-specific parsed data
  for (const s of sections) {
    if (s.id === "icymi") {
      s.entries = parseIcymi(s.content);
    }
    if (s.id === "judge") {
      s.rows = parseJudge(s.content);
    }
  }

  const digest = {
    ...parsed.metadata,
    sections,
    _salvaged: parsed._salvaged,
    // The full raw agent response (all blocks: <notes>, <draft>, <check>
    // and possibly <final>), kept alongside the rendered digest so failures
    // can be diagnosed without re-running the model (I7).
    raw: response,
  };

  // Carry figure metadata through so standalone template rendering can
  // resolve image URLs (figures are parsed at fetch time, not by the agent).
  digest.figures = source.figures || [];
  digest.baseUrl = source.baseUrl || "";

  return digest;
}

=== src/fetch.js ===

/**
 * Fetch pipeline for paper sources.
 *
 * Resolves a user-supplied source into HTML text plus asset metadata:
 *
 *  - an arXiv link / id -> the arXiv HTML version
 *    (https://arxiv.org/html/<id>), falling back to the abstract page only
 *    when no HTML version exists;
 *  - a general link -> fetched directly;
 *  - raw HTML text -> used as-is;
 *  - a local PDF path -> marked as pdf (caller decides how to extract text).
 *
 * HTML inputs may arrive with a folder of the same name holding assets, or
 * with inline image links (directly copied outerHTML). This module returns the
 * raw HTML together with a figure map so the template stage can prepend image
 * URLs.
 */

import { parseHTML } from "linkedom";

const USER_AGENT = "paper-digest/0.1 (https://github.com/lafqu/paper-digest)";

class FetchError extends Error {
  constructor(message) {
    super(message);
    this.name = "FetchError";
  }
}

const ARXIV_ID_RE = /(?:https?:\/\/)?(?:arxiv\.org\/(?:abs|html)\/)?(?<id>\d{4}\.\d{4,5}(?:v\d+)?)(?:\.pdf)?/i;

function isRawHtml(text) {
  const t = text.trimStart().toLowerCase();
  return t.startsWith("<!doctype html>") || t.startsWith("<html");
}

async function _fetch(url, { timeout = 30000 } = {}) {
  const controller = new AbortController();
  const timer = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), timeout);
  try {
    const resp = await fetch(url, {
      headers: { "User-Agent": USER_AGENT },
      signal: controller.signal,
    });
    if (!resp.ok) {
      throw new FetchError(`failed to fetch ${url}: ${resp.status} ${resp.statusText}`);
    }
    // Decode explicitly at the single place bytes become a string: honor the
    // declared charset, falling back to UTF-8 (never rely on the default).
    const buf = await resp.arrayBuffer();
    const charset = /charset=\s*([\w-]+)/i.exec(resp.headers.get("content-type") ?? "")?.[1];
    try {
      return new TextDecoder(charset ?? "utf-8").decode(buf);
    } catch (_) {
      return new TextDecoder("utf-8", { fatal: false }).decode(buf);
    }
  } finally {
    clearTimeout(timer);
  }
}

function extractArxivId(text) {
  const m = ARXIV_ID_RE.exec(text.trim());
  return m ? m.groups.id : null;
}

function arxivHtmlUrl(arxivId) {
  return `https://arxiv.org/html/${arxivId}`;
}

function stripTags(html) {
  return html.replace(/<[^>]+>/g, " ").replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
}

function parseTitle(html) {
  const m = /<h1 class="title mathjax">\s*Title:\s*(?<t>.*?)\s*<\/h1>/i.exec(html);
  return m ? stripTags(m.groups.t) : null;
}

const escapeRegExp = (s) => s.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, "\\$&");

/**
 * Serialise an inline <svg> element to markup that stands on its own.
 *
 * LaTeXML SVGs paint with CSS custom properties (e.g. --ltx-fill-color)
 * defined in the element's inline style and in arXiv's stylesheet. Without
 * that stylesheet `var(--ltx-fill-color)` is undefined, so resolve the
 * custom properties declared on the element itself to literal colours and
 * drop the now-resolved declarations.
 */
function serialiseSvg(el) {
  let markup = el.outerHTML;
  const styleM = /style="([^"]*)"/.exec(markup);
  const vars = {};
  if (styleM) {
    for (const part of styleM[1].split(";")) {
      const kv = part.split(":");
      if (kv.length === 2 && kv[0].trim().startsWith("--")) {
        vars[kv[0].trim()] = kv[1].trim();
      }
    }
  }
  if (Object.keys(vars).length > 0) {
    for (const [name, value] of Object.entries(vars)) {
      markup = markup.replace(
        new RegExp("\\bvar\\(" + escapeRegExp(name) + "\\)", "g"),
        value,
      );
    }
    markup = markup.replace(
      /style="([^"]*)"/,
      (_, style) => {
        const rest = style
          .split(";")
          .filter((p) => !p.trim().startsWith("--"))
          .join(";")
          .trim();
        return rest ? `style="${rest}"` : "";
      },
    );
  }
  return markup;
}

/**
 * Extract <figure> entries (real figures only, never tables).
 *
 * Supports two shapes:
 *  - arXiv/LaTeXML: <figure class="ltx_figure" id="S2.F1"><svg ...>...</svg><figcaption>...</figcaption></figure>
 *  - legacy/digest: <figure data-n="1" data-a="S2.F1"><figcaption>...</figcaption></figure>
 *
 * Each entry records the sequential index, the data-a id (or id attribute),
 * the caption, and either an image URL resolved against baseUrl (for ``
 * or `<object data>`) or the serialised inline `<svg>` markup. `ltx_table`
 * elements (and figures wrapping tables) are excluded.
 */
function parseFigures(html, baseUrl) {
  const { document } = parseHTML(html);

  const figures = [];
  let index = 0;

  for (const fig of document.querySelectorAll(
    "figure.ltx_figure, figure[data-n], figure[data-a]",
  )) {
    if (fig.classList.contains("ltx_table")) continue;

    const dataA = fig.getAttribute("data-a") || fig.getAttribute("id") || "";
    const nAttr = fig.getAttribute("data-n");
    if (!dataA && !nAttr) continue;

    index += 1;
    const n = nAttr || dataA || String(index);

    const captionEl = fig.querySelector("figcaption");
    const caption = captionEl
      ? captionEl.textContent.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim()
      : "";

    let url = "";
    let svg = "";

    const img = fig.querySelector("img");
    if (img) {
      const src = img.getAttribute("src");
      if (src) url = new URL(src, baseUrl).href;
    } else {
      const svgEl = fig.querySelector("svg");
      if (svgEl) {
        svg = serialiseSvg(svgEl);
      } else {
        const obj = fig.querySelector("object");
        const data = obj ? obj.getAttribute("data") : null;
        if (data) url = new URL(data, baseUrl).href;
      }
    }

    figures.push({ n, dataA, caption, url, svg });
  }
  return figures;
}

/**
 * Resolve *source* into a Source descriptor.
 *
 * @param {string} source - arxiv id/link, url, html text, or pdf path
 * @param {object} [opts]
 * @param {string} [opts.assetFolder] - folder beside an HTML file holding assets
 * @returns {Promise<{kind:string, value:string, html?:string, title?:string, authors:string[], figures:object[], baseUrl?:string, assetFolder?:string}>}
 */
export async function resolve(source, { assetFolder } = {}) {
  source = String(source).trim();

  // Raw HTML text.
  if (isRawHtml(source)) {
    return {
      kind: "html",
      value: source,
      html: source,
      title: parseTitle(source),
      authors: [],
      figures: parseFigures(source, "https://localhost/"),
      baseUrl: undefined,
      assetFolder,
    };
  }

  // Local PDF path.
  if (source.toLowerCase().endsWith(".pdf")) {
    const fs = await import("fs");
    if (fs.existsSync(source)) {
      return {
        kind: "pdf",
        value: source,
        title: undefined,
        authors: [],
        figures: [],
        baseUrl: undefined,
        assetFolder,
      };
    }
    throw new FetchError(`PDF not found: ${source}`);
  }

  const arxivId = extractArxivId(source);
  if (arxivId) {
    const htmlUrl = arxivHtmlUrl(arxivId);
    try {
      const html = await _fetch(htmlUrl);
      const figures = parseFigures(html, htmlUrl);
      return {
        kind: "arxiv",
        value: arxivId,
        html,
        title: parseTitle(html),
        authors: [],
        figures,
        baseUrl: htmlUrl,
        assetFolder,
      };
    } catch (err) {
      // Fall back to abstract page only; PDF handled separately by caller.
      const absHtml = await _fetch(`https://arxiv.org/abs/${arxivId}`);
      return {
        kind: "arxiv",
        value: arxivId,
        html: absHtml,
        title: parseTitle(absHtml),
        authors: [],
        figures: [],
        baseUrl: htmlUrl,
        assetFolder,
      };
    }
  }

  // General URL.
  const html = await _fetch(source);
  return {
    kind: "url",
    value: source,
    html,
    title: parseTitle(html),
    authors: [],
    figures: parseFigures(html, source),
    baseUrl: source,
    assetFolder,
  };
}

export { FetchError, parseFigures };

=== src/index.js ===

export const version = "0.1.0";

export const stages = [
  "fetch",
  "digest",
  "template",
];

export const outputDir = "outputs";

=== src/llm.js ===

/**
 * OpenRouter LLM client, modeled on the reference Python `LLM` class.
 *
 * Reads the API key from `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` or `~/.paper_digest_key`,
 * calls https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/chat/completions, tries configured
 * models in order with retries and a per-run request cap.
 */

import { homedir } from "os";
import { join, dirname } from "path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "url";
import { existsSync, readFileSync } from "fs";

const OPENROUTER_CHAT = "https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/chat/completions";
const OPENROUTER_KEY = "https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/key";
const KEY_FILE = join(homedir(), ".config/.paper_digest_key");

// Resolve the project config relative to this module's location (the package
// root) so `paper-digest digest` works no matter what the current working
// directory is. `process.cwd()` is kept as a fallback.
const SELF_DIR = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
const PKG_ROOT = join(SELF_DIR, "..");

const DEFAULT_CFG = {
  models: [],
  model: undefined,
  temperature: 0.2,
  // One response must hold notes, draft, check and possibly final. Keep
  // the cap comfortably above a single digest's size (~2-3k tokens).
  max_tokens: 6144,
  retries: 2,
  max_requests_per_run: 50,
  timeout_s: 30,
};

export class LLMUnavailable extends Error {
  constructor(message, options) {
    super(message, options);
    this.name = "LLMUnavailable";
  }
}

function loadKey() {
  const env = process.env.OPENROUTER_API_KEY;
  if (env) return env;
  if (existsSync(KEY_FILE)) {
    try {
      return readFileSync(KEY_FILE, "utf8").trim();
    } catch (_) {
      return "";
    }
  }
  return "";
}

function loadConfigFile() {
  for (const base of [PKG_ROOT, process.cwd()]) {
    const path = join(base, "paper-digest.config.json");
    if (!existsSync(path)) continue;
    try {
      return JSON.parse(readFileSync(path, "utf8"));
    } catch (_) {
      return null;
    }
  }
  return null;
}

export function loadConfig(cfg = {}) {
  const merged = { ...DEFAULT_CFG, ...cfg };
  const fileCfg = loadConfigFile();
  if (fileCfg) Object.assign(merged, fileCfg);
  if (process.env.PAPER_DIGEST_MODEL) {
    merged.models = [process.env.PAPER_DIGEST_MODEL];
  }
  let models = merged.models || (merged.model ? [merged.model] : []);
  models = models.filter(Boolean);
  merged.models = models;
  return merged;
}

/**
 * Best-effort extraction of a JSON object from model output. Strips fenced
 * code blocks, fixes invalid backslash escape sequences (common when agents
 * emit LaTeX such as \hat or over-escaped \\\\(\\), removes trailing commas,
 * and, if needed, slices between the first `{` and last `}`. Sets
 * `_salvaged: true` when the raw text needed repair. Throws when no object can
 * be recovered so the caller can retry with a correction hint.
 */
export function extractJson(content) {
  let text = String(content).trim();
  let salvaged = false;
  const markSalvaged = () => { salvaged = true; };

  const fence =
    /```json\n([\s\S]*?)\n```/i.exec(text) || /```\n([\s\S]*?)\n```/i.exec(text);
  if (fence) {
    text = fence[1].trim();
    markSalvaged();
  }

  // JSON \f is form feed (U+000C), which breaks LaTeX \frac etc. Replace a
  // standalone \f (not preceded by backslash) with \\f so JSON.parse yields
  // \f (backslash + f) instead of a form feed character.
  const beforeFf = text.length;
  text = text.replace(/(?<!\\)\\f/g, "\\\\f");
  if (text.length !== beforeFf) markSalvaged();

  // Fix invalid backslash escape sequences. JSON only allows \" \\ \/ \b \f
  // \n \r \t \uXXXX after a backslash. Agents often emit LaTeX like \hat,
  // \mathrm, \(...\) with a single or wrong number of backslashes, and may
  // write \\" inside a string when they mean a literal quote. Walk the text
  // left to right: valid escape sequences are preserved verbatim; an invalid
  // \X is re-encoded as \\X; and \\" followed by content (not a string
  // terminator) is re-encoded as \\\" so the quote stays inside the string.
  const VALID_ESCAPES = '"\\/bfnrtu';
  const STRUCTURAL = ":,}]\"\t\n\r ";
  let fixed = "";
  let i = 0;
  let changed = false;
  while (i < text.length) {
    if (text[i] === "\\" && i + 1 < text.length) {
      const next = text[i + 1];
      if (VALID_ESCAPES.includes(next)) {
        if (next === "\\" && i + 2 < text.length && text[i + 2] === '"') {
          // Skip whitespace to decide if the quote is content or a terminator.
          let j = i + 3;
          while (j < text.length && " \t\n\r".includes(text[j])) j += 1;
          const afterWs = text[j];
          const isContent =
            afterWs !== undefined && !":,}]\"".includes(afterWs);
          if (isContent) {
            // \\" -> \\\" so the quote stays inside the string
            fixed += '\\\\\\"';
            i += 3;
            changed = true;
            continue;
          }
        }
        fixed += "\\" + next;
        i += 2;
      } else {
        fixed += "\\\\" + next;
        i += 2;
        changed = true;
      }
    } else {
      fixed += text[i];
      i += 1;
    }
  }
  if (changed) {
    text = fixed;
    markSalvaged();
  }

  // Salvage unescaped double quotes inside string values — a common LLM slip
  // (e.g. writing ""title" author" instead of \"title\" author). Walk the
  // text tracking string state; an unescaped quote inside a string that is
  // followed by a non-structural character is content, not a terminator.
  let unescapedFixed = "";
  let inString = false;
  let uChanged = false;
  let u = 0;
  while (u < text.length) {
    const c = text[u];
    if (inString) {
      if (c === "\\") {
        unescapedFixed += c;
        if (u + 1 < text.length) unescapedFixed += text[u + 1];
        u += 2;
        continue;
      }
      if (c === '"') {
        let j = u + 1;
        while (j < text.length && " \t\n\r".includes(text[j])) j += 1;
        const after = text[j];
        const isTerminator =
          after === undefined || ",}]".includes(after) || after === ":";
        if (isTerminator) {
          unescapedFixed += c;
          inString = false;
        } else {
          unescapedFixed += '\\"';
          uChanged = true;
        }
        u += 1;
        continue;
      }
      unescapedFixed += c;
      u += 1;
    } else {
      unescapedFixed += c;
      if (c === '"') inString = true;
      u += 1;
    }
  }
  if (uChanged) {
    text = unescapedFixed;
    markSalvaged();
  }

  let parsed = null;
  try {
    parsed = JSON.parse(text);
  } catch (_) {
    markSalvaged();
    // Remove trailing commas before } or ] — a common LLM slip.
    const commaFixed = text.replace(/,\s*([}\]])/g, "$1");
    try {
      parsed = JSON.parse(commaFixed);
    } catch (__) {
      // Slice between the first { and last } to drop surrounding prose.
      const first = commaFixed.indexOf("{");
      const last = commaFixed.lastIndexOf("}");
      if (first !== -1 && last !== -1 && last > first) {
        try {
          parsed = JSON.parse(commaFixed.slice(first, last + 1));
        } catch (___ ) {
          parsed = null;
        }
      }
    }
  }
  if (!parsed) {
    throw new Error("no parseable JSON object in response");
  }

  // JSON \f is form feed (U+000C), which breaks LaTeX \frac etc. Walk the
  // parsed object and replace any form feed chars in string values with f
  // (the preceding backslash, if any, is already present from \\ in the
  // JSON source).
  const FF = "\f";
  function fixFormFeeds(obj) {
    if (typeof obj === "string") {
      if (obj.includes(FF)) {
        salvaged = true;
        return obj.split(FF).join("f");
      }
      return obj;
    }
    if (Array.isArray(obj)) return obj.map(fixFormFeeds);
    if (obj && typeof obj === "object") {
      const out = {};
      for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(obj)) out[k] = fixFormFeeds(v);
      return out;
    }
    return obj;
  }
  parsed = fixFormFeeds(parsed);

  return { _salvaged: salvaged, ...parsed };
}

/**
 * Extract a Markdown document with YAML frontmatter from model output.
 *
 * Strips markdown fences, parses the frontmatter block (--- ... ---) into
 * metadata, and returns the body text. Sets _salvaged: true when the raw text
 * needed repair. Throws when no frontmatter can be found.
 */
export function extractMarkdown(content) {
  // Normalize CRLF to LF so frontmatter parsing works regardless of platform.
  let text = String(content).replace(/\r\n/g, "\n").replace(/\r/g, "\n").trim();
  let salvaged = false;
  const markSalvaged = () => { salvaged = true; };

  const fence =
    /```markdown\n([\s\S]*?)\n```/i.exec(text) ||
    /```\n([\s\S]*?)\n```/i.exec(text);
  if (fence) {
    text = fence[1].trim();
    markSalvaged();
  }

  // Parse YAML frontmatter: --- ... ---
  const fmRe = /^---\n([\s\S]*?)\n---\n([\s\S]*)$/;
  let m = fmRe.exec(text);

  if (!m && text.startsWith("---\n")) {
    // Frontmatter opened with --- but never closed. Salvage by treating
    // everything up to the first real section header as frontmatter, then the
    // rest as body. Corrupted frontmatter fields that the agent wrote as
    // markdown headers (e.g. "## title: ...") are demoted back to fields.
    const sectionTitles = new Set([
      "motivation",
      "core idea",
      "distinguishing factor",
      "evaluation and conclusions",
      "in case you missed it",
      "cautious judgement",
      "sources and limits",
    ]);
    const rest = text.slice(4); // drop the leading "---\n"
    const lines = rest.split("\n");
    let splitIdx = lines.length;
    for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
      const hm = /^##\s+(.+)$/.exec(lines[i]);
      if (!hm) continue;
      const t = hm[1]
        .trim()
        .replace(/\s*\([^)]*confidence[^)]*\)\s*$/i, "")
        .trim()
        .toLowerCase();
      if (sectionTitles.has(t)) {
        splitIdx = i;
        break;
      }
    }
    const fmLines = lines.slice(0, splitIdx).map((l) => l.replace(/^##\s+/, ""));
    m = [text, fmLines.join("\n"), lines.slice(splitIdx).join("\n")];
    markSalvaged();
  }

  if (!m) {
    throw new Error("no parseable Markdown frontmatter in response");
  }

  const fmText = m[1];
  const body = m[2];
  const metadata = {};
  for (const line of fmText.split("\n")) {
    const colon = line.indexOf(":");
    if (colon === -1) continue;
    const key = line.slice(0, colon).trim();
    let val = line.slice(colon + 1).trim();
    if (!key) continue;
    // Try JSON.parse for values that look like JSON (arrays/objects).
    if ((val.startsWith("[") || val.startsWith("{")) && (val.endsWith("]") || val.endsWith("}"))) {
      try { val = JSON.parse(val); } catch (_) { /* keep as string */ }
    } else if (key === "authors" && val.includes(",")) {
      // Only authors is a list. All other scalar fields (verdict, whatRead,
      // ...) round-trip verbatim so commas inside prose keep their spaces.
      val = val.split(",").map((s) => s.trim()).filter(Boolean);
    }
    metadata[key] = val;
  }

  return { _salvaged: salvaged, metadata, body, raw: text };
}

function sleep(ms) {
  return new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));
}

export class LLM {
  constructor(cfg) {
    this.cfg = loadConfig(cfg);
    this.requests = 0;
    this.models = [];
    this.key = loadKey();
    if (!this.key) {
      throw new LLMUnavailable(
        `no API key (env OPENROUTER_API_KEY or ${KEY_FILE})`
      );
    }
  }

  async _http(url, body, timeout = 30) {
    const data = body !== null ? JSON.stringify(body) : null;
    const controller = new AbortController();
    const timer = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), timeout * 1000);
    try {
      const resp = await fetch(url, {
        method: body ? "POST" : "GET",
        headers: {
          Authorization: `Bearer ${this.key}`,
          "Content-Type": "application/json",
          "X-Title": "paper-digest",
        },
        body: data,
        signal: controller.signal,
      });
      const text = await resp.text();
      if (!resp.ok) {
        const err = new Error(`HTTP ${resp.status}`);
        err.name = "HTTPError";
        err.code = resp.status;
        err.detail = text;
        err.headers = resp.headers;
        throw err;
      }
      let parsed = null;
      try {
        parsed = JSON.parse(text);
      } catch (_) {
        parsed = {};
      }
      return [parsed, resp];
    } finally {
      clearTimeout(timer);
    }
  }

  async remainingDaily() {
    try {
      const [d] = await this._http(OPENROUTER_KEY, null, 30);
      return d?.data?.free_model_daily_requests?.remaining ?? null;
    } catch (e) {
      console.warn(
        `[paper-digest] could not read free-request quota (${
          e.constructor.name
        }); continuing`
      );
      return null;
    }
  }

  async chat(system, user) {
    const c = this.cfg;
    const warn = (msg) => console.warn(`[paper-digest] ${msg}`);
    let models = c.models;
    if (!models || !models.length) {
      if (c.model) models = [c.model];
      else throw new LLMUnavailable("no models configured");
    }
    let last = "unknown";

    for (const model of models) {
      const body = {
        model,
        temperature: c.temperature,
        max_tokens: c.max_tokens,
        messages: [
          { role: "system", content: system },
          { role: "user", content: user },
        ],
      };
      for (let attempt = 0; attempt <= c.retries; attempt++) {
        if (this.requests >= c.max_requests_per_run) {
          throw new LLMUnavailable(`per-run request cap reached (${last})`);
        }
        this.requests += 1;
        try {
          const [resp] = await this._http(
            OPENROUTER_CHAT,
            body,
            c.timeout_s
          );
          const choices = resp.choices || [];
          if (!choices.length) {
            throw new Error(
              `response has no choices (response_id=${
                resp.id ?? "?"
              }, model=${resp.model ?? model}, keys=${Object.keys(resp)
                .sort()
                .join(", ")})`
            );
          }
          const choice = choices[0];
          const message = choice.message || {};
          let content = (message.content || "").trim();
          if (!content) {
            throw new Error(
              `empty content (response_id=${resp.id ?? "?"}, model=${
                resp.model ?? model
              }, finish_reason=${choice.finish_reason ?? null}, refusal=${
                message.refusal ?? null
              }, usage=${JSON.stringify(resp.usage ?? null)})`
            );
          }
          this.models.push(resp.model ?? model);
          return content;
        } catch (e) {
          if (e instanceof LLMUnavailable) throw e;
          if (e.name === "HTTPError") {
            const detail = String(e.detail ?? "")
              .slice(0, 500)
              .trim();
            last = `HTTP ${e.code}` + (detail ? `: ${detail}` : "");
            warn(
              `model ${model} attempt ${attempt + 1}/${c.retries + 1} failed: ${last}`
            );
            if (e.code === 401) {
              throw new LLMUnavailable(last, { cause: e });
            }
            if ([400, 402, 403, 404].includes(e.code)) {
              break; // this model is unusable; try the next
            }
            const wait =
              e.code === 429
                ? parseInt(e.headers?.get("Retry-After") ?? "0", 10) || 0
                : 0;
            await sleep(Math.min(Math.max(wait, 5), 30) || 3);
            continue;
          }
          last = `${e.constructor.name}: ${e.message}`;
          warn(
            `model ${model} attempt ${attempt + 1}/${c.retries + 1} failed: ${last}`
          );
          await sleep(3);
        }
      }
    }
    throw new LLMUnavailable(`all configured models failed (${last})`);
  }
}

=== src/prompt.js ===

/**
 * Master prompt for the paper-digest agent.
 *
 * One request, no repair loop. To get loop-like behaviour inside that single
 * response the agent emits up to four blocks, in order:
 *   <notes>  evidence ledger (retrieval pass; ignored by the pipeline)
 *   <draft>  document written from the ledger
 *   <check>  adversarial audit of the draft against the ledger and the source
 *   <final>  corrected document, ONLY when the check found fixes
 * Only <final> (else <draft>) is consumed: a single Markdown document with YAML
 * frontmatter for metadata and markdown body for sections. No JSON, no escaping.
 * The Markdown is consumed by src/render.js to produce the digest HTML.
 *
 * There is deliberately no citation list: the Sources and limits section is a
 * single prose paragraph.
 */

function q(s) {
  return JSON.stringify(String(s));
}

/**
 * Pull the document out of the agent's response.
 * Takes the LAST complete <final>...</final> match. Returns null if there is
 * none -- which is not necessarily failure: per the OUTPUT CONTRACT the agent
 * omits <final> when its check is clean ("Fixes to apply: 0"), in which case
 * callers should fall back to the draft (see extractDraft). Null only signals
 * genuinely missing output (e.g. a truncated response with neither block).
 *
 * @param {string} response - raw agent output
 * @returns {string|null}
 */
export function extractFinal(response) {
  const matches = [...String(response).matchAll(/<final>([\s\S]*?)<\/final>/g)];
  if (matches.length === 0) return null;
  return matches[matches.length - 1][1].trim();
}

/**
 * Pull the draft document out of the agent's response.
 * Takes the LAST complete <draft>...</draft> match. Returns null if there is
 * none (for example, truncated output).
 *
 * @param {string} response - raw agent output
 * @returns {string|null}
 */
export function extractDraft(response) {
  const matches = [...String(response).matchAll(/<draft>([\s\S]*?)<\/draft>/g)];
  if (matches.length === 0) return null;
  return matches[matches.length - 1][1].trim();
}

/**
 * Pull the evidence ledger out of the agent's response (debugging only; the
 * pipeline does not render it). Returns null if absent.
 *
 * @param {string} response - raw agent output
 * @returns {string|null}
 */
export function extractNotes(response) {
  const matches = [...String(response).matchAll(/<notes>([\s\S]*?)<\/notes>/g)];
  if (matches.length === 0) return null;
  return matches[matches.length - 1][1].trim();
}

/**
 * Build the master prompt given the paper source text and figure metadata.
 *
 * @param {string} paperText - the extracted paper text (HTML stripped or PDF text)
 * @param {object[]} figures - { n, dataA, caption, src, url }
 * @param {string} baseUrl - the arXiv HTML URL actually read
 * @param {string} whatRead - note of what was read (full text / abstract only / ...)
 * @returns {string}
 */
export function buildMasterPrompt({ paperText, figures = [], baseUrl = "", whatRead = "" }) {
  const figureList = figures
    .map(
      (f, i) =>
        `    - index=${i + 1} data-n=${q(i + 1)} data-a=${q(f.dataA)} caption=${q(f.caption)} url=${q(f.url)}`
    )
    .join("\n")
    || "    (none)";

  return `You are a careful, skeptical research-paper reader. Write for a technical reader at graduate level in computer architecture and EDA: no simplifying, no padding, no restating the abstract. Every section should tell the reader something they could not get from the abstract alone. When the paper says something and you think otherwise, keep the two apart: what the paper reports versus "my read".

The digest exists so the reader can absorb a paper fast and know how far to trust it. Digests of different papers are compared side by side, so the structure is fixed.

READING BUDGET
A digest is a second-pass read, not a review. Read the paper once, carefully, then write. Do not go to a third pass unless explicitly asked.

Do not:
- inspect derivations or proofs;
- reformalize the paper's problem into equations it does not already contain;
- open, fetch, or search for any cited paper (including the authors' earlier version);
- check arithmetic, or cross-check numbers between tables, text, and figures.

Exception: cross-check a headline number (abstract, verdict, main results) only if something about it looks off; if confirming it is not trivial, say it looks suspicious and move on.

TRACEABILITY
Everything you write should be traceable to what the paper says or does on the page. A claim that needs outside work to establish is either stated as "not verified" or left out. Words that judge implementation quality or novelty ("unoptimized", "naive", "standard", "state of the art", "outperforms", "novel") need the paper's own wording or a named comparison behind them; otherwise drop the word. Do not attribute a difference in results to a cause (training recipe, implementation quality, data) unless the paper says so; if you suspect one, write it as "possibly" and say it is not verified.

SOURCE HANDLING
The source text below was machine-extracted from arXiv HTML. It may contain page chrome (issue-report widgets, footers, funder lists), garbled symbols, or math written twice (a rendered form followed by LaTeX). Ignore chrome. For math, use the clean LaTeX form and never copy garbled symbols. If garbling made part of the paper unreadable, say which part in "Sources and limits" and lower that section's confidence.

SOURCE TEXT (what was read: ${whatRead}):
\`\`\`
${paperText}
\`\`\`

FIGURES (arXiv HTML, file names and element ids recorded for the template):
${figureList}
BASE URL (the arXiv HTML version actually read): ${baseUrl}

OUTPUT CONTRACT
Your entire response consists of these blocks, in this order, and nothing else: no text before, between, or after them.

<notes>
EVIDENCE LEDGER. Plain text, at most 400 words. Do not write the document here.
