<h1 class="ui text-3xl font-semibold leading-tight mt-1">LocalTrain: Integer-Only Local Learning for Energy-Efficient CNN Training on Edge FPGAs</h1>
<p class="ui text-sm mt-1" style="color:var(--mute)">Shiqi Ding, Xuanyu Liang, Xinyang Liu, Boyang Li, Zhiqiang Que, Guosheng Hu, Jiayang Li</p>
<p class="mt-4"><b>Verdict.</b> The paper reports a working KV260 prototype that trains a four-layer CNN and its local classifiers from scratch with integer arithmetic on one shared matrix engine. The energy advantage over an integer-method Jetson run is measured under different power boundaries,and the mechanism claims (compact errors,tile folding,direct conversion) rest on logical-capacity accounting and isolated RTL tests rather than board-level ablations. My read is that it is credible as a feasibility demonstration and weak as attribution of gains to individual mechanisms. <span class="conf">76%</span></p>
</header>

<h2 class="s" id="mot">Motivation<span class="conf">80%</span></h2><div class="prose"><p>The paper argues that on-device training is bottlenecked by computation, retained forward activations, and data movement. Local learning (a per-layer objective, no cross-layer error propagation) and integer arithmetic each address part of this, and NITRO-D and FF-INT8 show integer local training works on GPUs. The authors say the open question is hardware: how convolution and local-classifier operations should share resources, and how saved operands, gradients, and quantization scales should be stored and reused. They add that local learning still needs operand retention, gradient accumulation, and scale selection.</p>
<p>My read: the gap is real and specific. Earlier FPGA trainers share engines across forward, backward, and weight-gradient passes, but not across local-classifier work. The motivation is less convincing on the energy side, since the baseline is the authors&#39; own integer implementation on a GPU.</p>
</div><h2 class="s" id="core">Core idea<span class="conf">85%</span></h2><div class="prose"><p>Each of four convolutional layers (alternating standard and grouped 3×3, bias-free) has a trainable local classifier on its quantized ReLU outputs. Each channel is averaged over four spatial regions, giving \(4C\) features per layer rather than \(HWC\). With regional features \(F\), pre-update classifier weights \(V_{\mathrm{pre}}\), and classifier errors \(E\), all three classifier computations are matrix products that run on the same engine as the convolutions:</p>
<p>\[Z = F V_{\mathrm{pre}}^{T},\qquad G_V = E^{T}F,\qquad E_F = E V_{\mathrm{pre}}\]</p>
<p>Regional errors \(E_F\) stay compact in DDR. They are broadcast over their region on chip only when the convolutional weight gradient needs spatial errors. The \(1/A\) averaging factor is folded into the exponent, and a ReLU mask is applied. Training finishes one layer before the next, so saved inputs can be reused afterward.</p>
<p>Numerics are shared-exponent INT8 with INT32 accumulation. Conversion divides every code in a tensor by one power of two:</p>
<p>\[q_i' = \operatorname{sat}_8\!\big(\operatorname{RN}(q_i 2^{-s})\big),\qquad e' = e + s,\quad s\ge 0\]</p>
<p>Convolutional output scales come from history rather than the current batch. After eight warmup batches, a histogram of raw INT32 magnitudes gives the 99.9th-percentile bin bound \(U_t\), a candidate exponent \(h_t\), and a target that is the maximum over the previous four batches:</p>
<p>\[h_t = e_{\mathrm{raw},t} + \min\{s\ge 0 : U_t \le 127\cdot 2^{s}\},\qquad e_{\mathrm{target},t}=\max_{i=1,\dots,4} h_{t-i}\]</p>
<p>This lets INT32-to-INT8 conversion happen directly as outputs stream out, while the histogram observes the raw codes in parallel.</p>
<p>Weight gradients are normalized per tensor by GB5, which targets a 5-bit magnitude, \(s_G=\max(0,\,b(\max|G|)-5)\). GB5 needs the complete batch gradient range before any update. The hardware therefore folds 32 partial gradient tensors (for \(B=256\), eight-sample groups) one 8×8 tile at a time through a 48-bit accumulator of 384 bytes of logical storage, with two 256-byte prefetch buffers.</p>
<p>The engine has eight pods of 8×8 INT8 outer-product tiles, so 512 MAC/cycle and a 102.4 GMAC/s peak at 200 MHz. The final classifier uses an INT16 master weight state \(M=16W\) so updates smaller than one INT8 step are kept. Classification errors use an FPGA-friendly adaptation of NITI&#39;s TiLoss, with lookup tables, one shared 28-fractional-bit reciprocal per sample, and a fixed \(2^{15}\) polynomial scale for small exponents.</p>
<figure data-n="1" data-a="S2.F1"><figcaption>Fig. 1: One CNN layer and its local supervision. Dashed paths retain operands; the local head also updates V. No convolutional input gradient is sent to earlier layers.</figcaption></figure><figure data-n="2" data-a="S3.F2"><figcaption>Fig. 2: Hardware organization. Convolution and classifier operations reuse the matrix engine. Regional errors expand in operand supply; numerical and output units share DDR access. Control is dashed; data connections are solid.</figcaption></figure></div><h2 class="s" id="diff">Distinguishing factor<span class="conf">70%</span></h2><div class="prose"><p>Against integer training algorithms (NITI, NITRO-D, FF-INT8): these are algorithm-level, mostly GPU results. The paper reuses NITI&#39;s TiLoss structure and its code-domain updates, and its new content is the hardware mapping and the FPGA-oriented rewrite of the loss (lookup tables, shared reciprocal, bounded polynomial). The training rules themselves are largely inherited.</p>
<p>Against FPGA backpropagation accelerators (EF-Train, Venkataramanaiah et al., Shao et al.): these share compute across forward, backward, and weight-gradient passes. Here the shared engine also absorbs local-classifier score, feature-error, and classifier-gradient products (Table I), and local supervision removes the cross-layer input-gradient path. The comparison in Table IV mixes six-layer narrow backprop networks with a four-layer wider, partly grouped local-learning network, so accuracy and energy are not like-for-like, and the paper says so.</p>
<p>Against historical-scaling work (Flexpoint, Yamaguchi et al.): the idea of using earlier batches to pick scales is adopted. The paper&#39;s specific choices are histogram percentiles, a four-batch window, and an eight-batch warmup, plus the hardware that makes direct conversion possible. Saturation is allowed and no clipping rate is guaranteed.</p>
<p>vs. their earlier version: the paper does not describe an earlier version in the text I read, and none was fetched.</p>
</div><h2 class="s" id="eval">Evaluation and conclusions<span class="conf">78%</span></h2><div class="prose"><p>Complete training on the KV260 at 200 MHz reaches 76.57% on CIFAR-10 (50 epochs) and 91.85% on SVHN (60 epochs), at about 4.20 W mean VCC_SOM power. Against the same integer method on a Jetson Orin Nano (25 W mode), measured energy is 40.4% and 42.1% lower, with 1.86× and 1.82× longer runtime. My read: this is the headline claim, and the boundaries differ. The FPGA measures VCC_SOM and the Jetson measures VDD_IN, with no idle subtraction, and the Jetson integer path is custom numerics in PyTorch, not an optimized kernel. The paper&#39;s own Jetson table shows Local FP32 on the Jetson at lower energy than the FPGA&#39;s CIFAR-10 figure, again under different boundaries. The FPGA&#39;s energy advantage therefore holds against the integer-on-GPU implementation specifically, not against a good GPU local-learning baseline. Functional agreement is supported by matched cumulative training predictions and final test counts between FPGA and Jetson integer runs, plus selected first-batch and checkpoint-restore state checks. The authors correctly say this is not every intermediate value.</p>
<p>Effective throughput is 31.22 and 31.57 GMAC/s, about 30% of the 102.4 peak. It is computed as nominal algorithmic MACs divided by measured PL time, and PL time is about 97% of wall time. The first two convolutions have short reductions (36 and 72 steps) against 128 result transfers for eight tiles, so result bandwidth is flagged as a constraint. My read: the number is honest about low utilization, but the stall attribution is a hypothesis, since the counters do not separate memory stalls from result backpressure.</p>
<p>Storage and conversion results are the mechanism evidence. Compact regional errors take 64 to 256 KiB per layer against 8 to 16 MiB for expanded INT8 spatial errors, and tile folding replaces a 432 KiB resident accumulator (conv 3) with 384 bytes plus 512 bytes of prefetch. Direct conversion avoids 448 MiB of raw-output write/read payload per full batch after warmup. In an isolated RTL test (262,144 INT32 values), direct conversion takes 50.0% fewer cycles with always-ready interfaces and 63.8% fewer under a fixed throttling model. My read: these are logical capacities and payload counts, not measured DDR traffic or peak live storage, and the reserved DDR workspace stays at 161.22 MiB. The module test excludes convolution and external scratch control. These mechanisms are plausible but not demonstrated on the board.</p>
<p>Software accuracy on the Jetson is somewhat surprising. Integer local (76.57% on CIFAR-10) beats Local FP32 (74.43%) but trails FP32 backprop (79.87%). Scheduled freezing cuts training time 28.4% and 32.3% while raising accuracy 6.38 and 2.03 points in a within-study comparison. My read: the integer-over-FP32 result mostly reflects differing training recipes (Adam and cross-entropy versus integer rules and scaling), which the authors partly acknowledge. The freezing gain means the schedule is part of the reported result, not a free speedup.</p>
<p>The paper&#39;s conclusion is that complete integer local CNN training works with shared arithmetic, that compact errors, folding, and historical scales reduce buffering, and that energy is lower though runtime is longer. It also states the evidence covers one FPGA seed, logical storage savings, and isolated conversion tests. My read: the evidence supports the feasibility and the energy-versus-Jetson-integer claim under stated caveats, and does not yet support attributing the gains to specific mechanisms.</p>
</div><h2 class="s" id="icymi">In case you missed it<span class="conf">75%</span></h2><div class="item"><span class="chip pos">+</span>The TiLoss rewrite is concrete: lookup tables for score exponents above -7, one reciprocal with 28 fractional bits shared across all classes per sample, a fixed \(2^{15}\) scale for exponents at or below -10, and stochastic division by 16 to reach INT8 errors.</div><div class="item"><span class="chip neu">·</span>Convolutional and local-classifier weights are updated in the code domain without aligning the gradient exponent to the fixed weight exponent; only the final classifier gets an INT16 master state (\(M=16W\), clipped to ±2032) that preserves sub-step updates.</div><div class="item"><span class="chip neg">−</span>The 64 MiB DDR buffer for raw outputs during warmup stays reserved afterward, so direct conversion removes traffic but not reserved capacity; total reserved DDR workspace stays at 161.22 MiB.</div><div class="item"><span class="chip neu">·</span>The histogram unit is 8 banks of 33 counters of 25 bits in LUTRAM with HLS initiation interval of one; the PS merges bank counts, checks the output count, and keeps the scale history. At evaluation, each layer's target is fixed to the maximum of its last four training candidates.</div><div class="item"><span class="chip neu">·</span>The deployed configuration bakes in layer freezing after 20/20/30/40 epochs, gradients with max magnitude at or below 1 are suppressed, and the final classifier's learning-rate shift changes from 24 to 25 after epoch 40.</div><div class="item"><span class="chip neg">−</span>The PS supplies all random values (dropout, stochastic rounding) through DDR and maintains scale history, so training is not fully self-contained on the PL; the SVHN run also drops one training image to fit eight-sample grouping.</div><h2 class="s" id="judge">Cautious judgement<span class="conf">65%</span></h2><div class="wrap"><table><thead><tr><th>Question</th><th>Answer</th><th>Conf.</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Fair comparison to external baselines?</td><td>Partly. The same integer method on Jetson is a controlled comparison, but power boundaries differ (VCC_SOM vs VDD_IN, no idle subtraction) and the Jetson integer path is unoptimized custom code. The FPGA literature table mixes different networks, precisions, and tool-estimated power, and the authors disclaim an energy ranking.</td><td>72%</td></tr><tr><td>Invents its own FoM?</td><td>No new headline metric. Effective GMAC/s is derived from nominal algorithmic MACs over PL time (excluding padding and non-matrix work), and logical storage reductions are capacity counts, not measured traffic.</td><td>78%</td></tr><tr><td>Rewrite of an existing idea?</td><td>Largely a hardware mapping of existing ideas: NITI-style integer training and TiLoss, local classifiers after Belilovsky et al., historical scaling after Flexpoint and Yamaguchi. The novelty is the shared-engine mapping, regional-error expansion, tile folding, and direct conversion.</td><td>68%</td></tr><tr><td>Reliable enough to reuse?</td><td>As a reference design for reimplementation, plausibly, given detailed numerics and state-matching checks. As evidence that each mechanism produces the stated gain, no: single FPGA seed, no board ablations, no stall breakdown, no code read.</td><td>60%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><h2 class="s" id="src">Sources and limits<span class="conf">90%</span></h2><div class="prose"><p>I read the full arXiv HTML text of v1 (main body, tables, and reference list). The two figures were identified by caption only and I did not view them. I did not read an appendix, supplementary material, or released code, and none of the cited papers were opened. I did not check derivations or arithmetic. I did not verify the isolated RTL results, the Table IV derived energies, or the claimed functional-agreement checks beyond what the paper reports. The extracted text contained some encoding noise in symbols; I relied on the clean LaTeX forms where present.</p>
</div>

const BASE = "";
const MAP = {};
document.querySelectorAll('figure[data-n]').forEach((f)=>{
  if (f.querySelector('img')) return;
  const n=f.dataset.n,img=new Image();img.loading='lazy';img.alt='Figure '+n;
  img.onerror=()=>{const a=document.createElement('a');a.href=BASE.replace(/\/$/,'')+'#'+f.dataset.a;a.textContent='Open Fig. '+n+' on arXiv';a.className='ui text-sm underline';a.style.color='var(--teal)';img.replaceWith(a);};
  img.src=MAP[n] ? new URL(MAP[n], BASE || window.location.origin).href : "";
  f.prepend(img);
});

<script>
/* Reading marker. R or a click on a sentence starts it; Up/Down (or k/j) move it; Esc or R stops it.
   Arrow keys scroll the page normally unless the marker is active. Runs after MathJax has typeset. */
(function(){
const BLK='main p:not(.cite),main li:not(.cite),main .item,main blockquote,main figcaption,main td';
const ALL=BLK+',main mjx-container[display="true"]';
const ABBR=/(?:\b(?:e\.g|i\.e|et al|etc|vs|cf|Figs?|Eqs?|Secs?|Tab|Refs?|Algs?|approx|resp|No)|\b[A-Z])\.$/;
const OBJ='\uFFFC';
let units=[],idx=-1,on=false,ready=false,hintEl;

function tokens(block){
  const items=[];let run=[];
  const flush=()=>{if(run.length)items.push({run});run=[];};
  const walk=n=>{
    for(let c=n.firstChild;c;c=c.nextSibling){
      if(c.nodeType===3){run.push({t:c});}
      else if(c.nodeType===1){
        const tag=c.tagName;
        if(tag==='MJX-CONTAINER'){
          if(c.getAttribute('display')==='true'){flush();items.push({disp:c});}
          else run.push({a:c});
        }else if(tag==='SCRIPT'||tag==='STYLE'||tag==='MJX-ASSISTIVE-MML'||tag==='IMG'){}
        else walk(c);
      }
    }
  };
  walk(block);flush();return items;
}

function sentences(run){
  let s='';const map=[];
  for(const p of run){
    if(p.t){const v=p.t.nodeValue;for(let i=0;i<v.length;i++)map.push({n:p.t,o:i,L:v.length});s+=v;}
    else{map.push({a:p.a});s+=OBJ;}
  }
  const cuts=[0];
  const re=/[.!?]+["'’”)\]]*(?=\s+[A-Z0-9"'‘(\[\uFFFC])/g;
  let m;
  while((m=re.exec(s))){const end=m.index+m[0].length;if(!ABBR.test(s.slice(Math.max(0,end-10),end)))cuts.push(end);}
  cuts.push(s.length);
  const out=[];
  for(let i=0;i<cuts.length-1;i++){
    let a=cuts[i],b=cuts[i+1];
    while(a<b&&/\s/.test(s[a]))a++;
    while(b>a&&/\s/.test(s[b-1]))b--;
    if(b>a)out.push([a,b]);
  }
  return {out,map};
}

function rangeFor(block,map,a,b){
  const r=document.createRange(),f=map[a],l=map[b-1];
  if(f.a)r.setStartBefore(f.a);
  else if(f.o===0){let n=f.n;while(n.parentNode!==block&&n===n.parentNode.firstChild)n=n.parentNode;r.setStartBefore(n);}
  else r.setStart(f.n,f.o);
  if(l.a)r.setEndAfter(l.a);
  else if(l.o===l.L-1){let n=l.n;while(n.parentNode!==block&&n===n.parentNode.lastChild)n=n.parentNode;r.setEndAfter(n);}
  else r.setEnd(l.n,l.o+1);
  return r;
}

function build(block){
  const out=[];
  for(const it of tokens(block)){
    if(it.disp){it.disp.classList.add('rd');out.push(it.disp);continue;}
    const {out:ss,map}=sentences(it.run),spans=[];
    for(let i=ss.length-1;i>=0;i--){
      const r=rangeFor(block,map,ss[i][0],ss[i][1]);
      const sp=document.createElement('span');sp.className='rd';
      sp.appendChild(r.extractContents());r.insertNode(sp);spans.push(sp);
    }
    out.push(...spans.reverse());
  }
  return out;
}

function hint(){
  if(!hintEl){hintEl=document.createElement('div');hintEl.id='rd-hint';document.body.appendChild(hintEl);}
  hintEl.textContent=on?'\u2191 \u2193 move \u00b7 Esc exit':'R: reading marker';
}
function inView(i){const r=units[i].getBoundingClientRect();return r.bottom>0&&r.top<innerHeight;}
function firstVisible(){for(let i=0;i<units.length;i++)if(units[i].getBoundingClientRect().top>=48)return i;return units.length-1;}
function ensure(u,fast){
  const r=u.getBoundingClientRect(),h=innerHeight;
  if(r.top<h*.18||r.bottom>h*.78)scrollBy({top:r.top-h*.38,behavior:fast?'auto':'smooth'});
}
function set(i,scroll,fast){
  if(units[idx])units[idx].classList.remove('on');
  idx=Math.max(0,Math.min(units.length-1,i));
  units[idx].classList.add('on');
  if(scroll)ensure(units[idx],fast);
}
function activate(i){
  on=true;document.body.classList.add('rd-active');
  set(i!==undefined?i:(idx>=0&&inView(idx)?idx:firstVisible()),i===undefined);hint();
}
function deactivate(){
  on=false;document.body.classList.remove('rd-active');
  if(units[idx])units[idx].classList.remove('on');hint();
}

document.addEventListener('keydown',e=>{
  if(!units.length||e.ctrlKey||e.metaKey||e.altKey)return;
  const t=e.target;if(t&&(/^(INPUT|TEXTAREA|SELECT)$/.test(t.tagName)||t.isContentEditable))return;
  if(e.key==='r'||e.key==='R'){e.preventDefault();on?deactivate():activate();return;}
  if(!on)return;
  if(e.key==='ArrowDown'||e.key==='j'){e.preventDefault();set(idx+1,true,e.repeat);}
  else if(e.key==='ArrowUp'||e.key==='k'){e.preventDefault();set(idx-1,true,e.repeat);}
  else if(e.key==='Escape')deactivate();
});
document.addEventListener('click',e=>{
  const sel=getSelection();if(sel&&!sel.isCollapsed)return;
  if(!units.length||e.target.closest('a,button,nav'))return;
  const u=e.target.closest('.rd');if(!u)return;
  const i=units.indexOf(u);if(i<0)return;
  if(on){set(i,false);}else activate(i);
});

function init(){
  if(ready)return;ready=true;
  for(const el of Array.from(document.querySelectorAll(ALL))){
    if(el.tagName==='MJX-CONTAINER'){if(!el.parentElement.closest(BLK)){el.classList.add('rd');units.push(el);}continue;}
    if(el.querySelector(BLK))continue;
    units.push(...build(el));
  }
  hint();
}
addEventListener('load',()=>{
  const p=window.MathJax&&window.MathJax.startup&&window.MathJax.startup.promise;
  Promise.resolve(p).then(init,init);
});
})();
</script>
</body>
</html>

=== test/run.js ===

#!/usr/bin/env node
/**
 * paper-digest pipeline tests.
 *
 * Plain-Node assert suite (no framework): one file, no network, runs against
 * the fixtures in test/fixtures/. Run from the repo root:
 *
 *   node test/run.js
 *
 * Exit code is non-zero if any assertion fails.
 */

import assert from "node:assert";
import fs from "node:fs";
import { join } from "node:path";

import { extractTextFromHtml } from "../src/cli.js";
import { parseFigures } from "../src/fetch.js";
import { render } from "../src/render.js";
import { extractMarkdown } from "../src/llm.js";
import { digestSource } from "../src/digest.js";
import { loadConfig } from "../src/llm.js";

const ROOT = join(process.cwd(), "test");
const FIX = join(ROOT, "fixtures");
const TMPL = join(process.cwd(), "assets", "template.html");

let passes = 0;
let failures = 0;

const pending = [];

function test(name, fn) {
  try {
    const result = fn();
    if (result instanceof Promise) {
      pending.push(
        result.then(
          () => {
            passes += 1;
            console.log("PASS " + name);
          },
          (e) => {
            failures += 1;
            console.log("FAIL " + name + " — " + e.message);
            console.log("   stack: " + (e.stack || "").split("\n").slice(0, 3).join(" | "));
          },
        ),
      );
    } else {
      passes += 1;
      console.log("PASS " + name);
    }
  } catch (e) {
    failures += 1;
    console.log("FAIL " + name + " — " + e.message);
  }
}

function read(f) {
  return fs.readFileSync(join(FIX, f), "utf8");
}

function renderHtml(digest, source = {}) {
  const { placeholders } = render(digest, source);
  let h = fs.readFileSync(TMPL, "utf8");
  for (const [token, value] of Object.entries(placeholders)) h = h.split(token).join(value);
  return h.replace("const MAP = {{MAP}};", "const MAP = {};");
}

const sectionsOrder = ["mot", "core", "diff", "eval", "icymi", "judge", "src"];

////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
// I1 — extracted source text
////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
test("I1 arxiv-sample: title first, no chrome before/after", () => {
  const out = extractTextFromHtml(read("arxiv-sample.html"));
  assert.ok(out.startsWith("# Title: Sample Paper on Local Learning"), "title is first: " + JSON.stringify(out.slice(0, 60)));
  const beforeTitle = out.slice(0, out.indexOf("Title:"));
  assert.ok(!/Report GitHub Issue|Funder:|nonprofit/.test(beforeTitle), "chrome before title: " + JSON.stringify(beforeTitle));
  const afterRefs = out.slice(out.indexOf("5 References"));
  assert.ok(!/Instructions for reporting errors|Report GitHub Issue/.test(afterRefs), "chrome after references");
});

test("I1 arxiv-sample: Table II region serialised as pipe rows", () => {
  const out = extractTextFromHtml(read("arxiv-sample.html"));
  assert.ok(out.includes("Table II: Ablation results."), "Table II caption present");
  assert.ok(out.includes("| Setting | CIFAR-10 | SVHN |"), "Table II header row");
  assert.ok(out.includes("| No folding | 74.00 | 90.00 |"), "Table II body row");
});

test("I1 localtrain: first 60 lines (see report for output)", () => {
  const out = extractTextFromHtml(read("localtrain.html"));
  const lines = out.split("\n").slice(0, 60);
  assert.ok(lines.length > 0, "extractor produced output");
  assert.ok(lines.some((l) => /# LocalTrain/.test(l)), "title present");
  console.log("    --- first 60 lines of localtrain extraction ---");
  console.log("    " + lines.join("\n    "));
  console.log("    --- end ---");
});

test("I1 no mojibake: non-ASCII (× ≈ ±) survives extraction", () => {
  assert.strictEqual(extractTextFromHtml("<p>1.86 \u00d7, \u2248, \u00b1</p>"), "1.86 ×, ≈, ±");
});

test("I1 each inline equation appears once (<math alttext>)", () => {
  const html =
    "<article class='ltx_document'><p>Gain <math alttext='1.86 \\times'>1.86 times</math> and risk <math alttext='s \\ge 0'>s &ge; 0</math>.</p></article>";
  const out = extractTextFromHtml(html);
  const eq1 = "\\(1.86 \\times\\)";
  const eq2 = "\\(s \\ge 0\\)";
  assert.strictEqual(out.split(eq1).length - 1, 1, "first equation once");
  assert.strictEqual(out.split(eq2).length - 1, 1, "second equation once");
  assert.ok(!out.includes("1.86 times"), "rendered form dropped");
  assert.ok(!out.includes("&ge;"), "rendered form dropped");
});

////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
// I2 — figure list and rendering
////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
test("I2 localtrain: figure list has exactly two entries", () => {
  const figs = parseFigures(read("localtrain.html"), "https://localhost/");
  assert.strictEqual(figs.length, 2, "count: " + JSON.stringify(figs));
  assert.deepStrictEqual(figs[0], { n: "1", dataA: "S2.F1", caption: "Fig. 1: One CNN layer and its local supervision. Dashed paths retain operands; the local head also updates V. No convolutional input gradient is sent to earlier layers.", url: "", svg: "" });
  assert.deepStrictEqual(figs[1].n, "2");
  assert.deepStrictEqual(figs[1].dataA, "S3.F2");
  assert.strictEqual(figs[1].url, "");
});

test("I2 arxiv-sample: figures only, tables excluded, SVG serialised, URLs resolved", () => {
  const figs = parseFigures(read("arxiv-sample.html"), "https://arxiv.org/html/2610.17099");
  assert.strictEqual(figs.length, 3, "count: " + JSON.stringify(figs));
  assert.ok(figs.every((f) => !/TABLE/i.test(f.caption)), "no tables listed");
  const f1 = figs[0];
  assert.strictEqual(f1.n, "S2.F1");
  assert.strictEqual(f1.dataA, "S2.F1");
  assert.strictEqual(f1.url, "");
  assert.ok(f1.svg.includes('fill="#0E6B73"'), "CSS custom property resolved to literal colour: " + f1.svg);
  assert.ok(f1.svg.includes('stroke="#16252C"'), "stroke colour resolved");
  assert.strictEqual(figs[1].url, "https://arxiv.org/html/fig2.pdf", "object data resolved");
  assert.strictEqual(figs[2].url, "https://arxiv.org/html/fig3.png", "img src resolved");
});

test("I2 render: inline-SVG figure inlined into the figure, scaled to column width", async () => {
  const md = read("handwritten.md");
  const { metadata, body } = extractMarkdown(md);
  const sections = [];
  // reuse splitSections via a tiny inline re-implementation to avoid coupling:
  const { splitSections } = await import("../src/digest.js");
  const all = splitSections(body);
  const digest = { ...metadata, sections: all, body, raw: md };
  const svg = '<svg width="400" height="200"><rect width="400" height="200" fill="#0E6B73"/><text x="50" y="100" fill="white">Fig. 1 diagram</text></svg>';
  const h = renderHtml(digest, {
    figures: [{ n: "1", dataA: "S2.F1", caption: "Fig. 1: diagram", url: "", svg }],
    baseUrl: "https://arxiv.org/html/2601.00001",
  });
  const f1 = h.slice(h.indexOf('data-n="1"'), h.indexOf("</figure>", h.indexOf('data-n="1"')));
  assert.ok(f1.includes("<svg"), "SVG inlined");
  assert.ok(/<svg width="100%" height="auto"/.test(f1), "scaled to column width");
  assert.ok(/viewBox="0 0 400 200"/.test(f1), "viewBox added so it scales");
  assert.ok(f1.includes("Fig. 1 diagram"), "svg content retained");
});

    body: "",
    raw: "",
  };
  const h = renderHtml(digest, {

});

  const { splitSections } = await import("../src/digest.js");

  for (const id of sectionsOrder) {
    const n = (h.match(new RegExp('id="' + id + '"', "g")) || []).length;
    assert.strictEqual(n, 1, id + " section appears once (got " + n + ")");
  }
  const srcText = "I read the full arXiv HTML text including tables and references.";

test("I4 render: Evaluation section has four .item blocks and no .prose wrapper", async () => {

test("I5 frontmatter: commas keep their following space; only authors is a list", () => {
  const fm =
    "---\ntitle: T\nauthors: A, B\nvenue: v\nid: 1\nwhatRead: full text, no code, no supplementary material\nverdict: The paper is good, and it shows (boundaries, and mechanisms (compact, tiled)), end.\noverallConfidence: 80\n---\n\n## Motivation (Confidence: 80%)\n\nbody.\n";
  const { metadata } = extractMarkdown(fm);
  assert.strictEqual(typeof metadata.verdict, "string", "verdict must stay scalar");
  assert.strictEqual(
    metadata.verdict,
    "The paper is good, and it shows (boundaries, and mechanisms (compact, tiled)), end.",
  );
  assert.strictEqual(metadata.whatRead, "full text, no code, no supplementary material");
  assert.deepStrictEqual(metadata.authors, ["A", "B"]);
  const digest = { ...metadata, sections: [], body: "body.", raw: fm };
  const h = renderHtml(digest, { figures: [], baseUrl: "" });
  assert.ok(h.includes("boundaries, and mechanisms"), "comma + space preserved in rendered verdict");
});

////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
// I6 — math and line breaks
////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
test("I6 render: display math -> \\[...\\], inline \\(...\\) untouched, \\\\ preserved, single newline joined", async () => {
  const content =
    "Inline: \\(x_i \\le y\\) and \\(a < b\\).\n\n$$\n\\begin{aligned}\nh_t &= 1,\\\\\\\\ne_t &= 2.\\\\\n\\end{aligned}\n$$\n\nA paragraph with a single newline\ncontinues on the next line as one paragraph.";
  const { splitSections } = await import("../src/digest.js");
  const digest = {
    title: "T", authors: ["A"], venue: "v", id: "1", whatRead: "w", verdict: "v", overallConfidence: 80,
    sections: [{ id: "core", title: "Core idea", content, confidence: 80 }],
    body: "",
    raw: "",
  };
  const h = renderHtml(digest, { figures: [], baseUrl: "" });
  const coreStart = h.indexOf('id="core"');
  const coreEnd = h.indexOf('id="', coreStart + 1);
  const slice = h.slice(coreStart, coreEnd > -1 ? coreEnd : h.length);
  assert.ok(slice.includes("\\["), "display math starts with \\[");
  assert.ok(slice.includes("\\begin{aligned}"), "display math contains \\begin{aligned}");
  assert.ok(slice.includes("\\end{aligned}"), "display math contains \\end{aligned}");
  assert.ok(slice.includes("\\]"), "display math ends with \\]");
  assert.ok(slice.includes("\\\\"), "multi-line \\\\ line breaks preserved");
  assert.ok(slice.includes("\\(x_i \\le y\\)"), "inline \\( ... \\) passes through (MathJax sees the original via the DOM)");
  assert.ok(slice.includes("\\(a &lt; b\\)"), "raw < inside inline math is escaped in HTML (MathJax sees the original via the DOM)");
  assert.ok(
    /<p>A paragraph with a single newline\ncontinues on the next line as one paragraph\.<\/p>/.test(slice),
    "single-newline paragraph stays one paragraph",
  );
});

test("I6 render: < and & inside math are escaped for HTML", async () => {
  const content = "Inline: \\(a < b \\& c\\)). Display: $$x = y \\& z$$.";
  const digest = {
    title: "T", authors: ["A"], venue: "v", id: "1", whatRead: "w", verdict: "v", overallConfidence: 80,
    sections: [{ id: "core", title: "Core idea", content, confidence: 80 }],
    body: "",
    raw: "",
  };
  const h = renderHtml(digest, { figures: [], baseUrl: "" });
  const slice = h.slice(h.indexOf('id="core"'), h.indexOf("</main>"));
  assert.ok(slice.includes("\\(a &lt; b \\&amp; c\\)"), "inline math escaped");
  assert.ok(slice.includes("\\[x = y \\&amp; z\\]"), "display math escaped");
  const pStart = slice.indexOf("<p>");
  const pEnd = slice.indexOf("</p>", pStart);
  const pInner = slice.slice(pStart + 3, pEnd);
  assert.ok(!/<[a-z]/.test(pInner), "no raw < tag from math");
});

////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
// I7 — output contract
////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
test("I7 runner: extractFinal then extractDraft fallback, truncation reported, fix-warning, rawResponse carried", async () => {
  const FM =
    "---\ntitle: t\nauthors: a\nvenue: v\nid: 1\nwhatRead: w\nverdict: v\noverallConfidence: 80\n---\n\n## Motivation (Confidence: 80%)\n\nbody.";
  const warnings = [];
  const origWarn = console.warn;
  console.warn = (m) => warnings.push(String(m));
  const source = { paperText: "x", figures: [], baseUrl: "", whatRead: "x" };
  try {
    const d1 = await digestSource(source, { client: { chat: async () => "<notes>n</notes><draft>" + FM + "</draft><check>Fixes to apply: 0</check><final>" + FM + "</final>" } });
    assert.ok(d1.raw.includes("<notes>") && d1.raw.includes("<draft>") && d1.raw.includes("<check>") && d1.raw.includes("<final>"), "raw stores all blocks");
    const d2 = await digestSource(source, { client: { chat: async () => "<notes>n</notes><draft>" + FM + "</draft><check>Fixes to apply: 0</check>" } });
    assert.ok(d2.raw.includes("<notes>") && d2.raw.includes("<draft>") && d2.raw.includes("<check>"), "raw stores all blocks when no final");
    assert.ok(!d2.raw.includes("<final>"), "no <final> in raw when check is clean");
    let threw = false;
    try {
      await digestSource(source, { client: { chat: async () => "<notes>n</notes><draft>" + FM.slice(0, 10) } });
    } catch (e) {
      threw = true;
      assert.ok(/no <final> or <draft> block/.test(e.message), "truncation reported: " + e.message);
    }
    assert.ok(threw, "truncated response must throw");
    warnings.length = 0;
    const d4 = await digestSource(source, { client: { chat: async () => "<notes>n</notes><draft>" + FM + "</draft><check>Fixes to apply: 2</check>" } });
    assert.ok(d4.raw.includes(FM.trimEnd()), "draft document preserved inside raw response");
    assert.ok(warnings.some((w) => /Fixes to apply/.test(w)), "warning mentions Fixes to apply: " + warnings.join("|"));
    console.warn = origWarn;
  } finally {
    console.warn = origWarn;
  }
});

test("I7 max_tokens high enough for notes+draft+check+final", () => {
  assert.ok(loadConfig().max_tokens >= 4096, "max_tokens = " + loadConfig().max_tokens);
});

test("I7 cli runDigest stores raw response alongside the digest", async () => {
  const FM =
    "---\ntitle: t\nauthors: a\nvenue: v\nid: 1\nwhatRead: w\nverdict: v\noverallConfidence: 80\n---\n\n## Motivation (Confidence: 80%)\n\nbody.\n## Core idea (Confidence: 80%)\n\nc.\n## Distinguishing factor (Confidence: 80%)\n\nd.\n## Evaluation and conclusions (Confidence: 80%)\n\n**R.** r.\n## In case you missed it (Confidence: 80%)\n\n- [pos] e.\n## Cautious judgement (Confidence: 80%)\n\n| Question | Answer | Confidence |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Fair comparison to external baselines? | x | 80% |\n| Invents its own FoM? | x | 80% |\n| Rewrite of an existing idea? | x | 80% |\n| Reliable enough to reuse? | x | 80% |\n## Sources and limits (Confidence: 80%)\n\ns.\n";
  const response = "<notes>ledger</notes><draft>" + FM + "</draft><check>Fixes to apply: 0</check><final>" + FM + "</final>";
  const htmlSource =
    "<!doctype html><html><head><meta charset='utf-8'><title>x</title></head><body><article class='ltx_document'><h1 class='title mathjax'>Title: T</h1><p>body</p></article></body></html>";
  const { runDigest } = await import("../src/cli.js");
  await fs.promises.rm(join(process.cwd(), "test", "tmp-out"), { recursive: true, force: true });
  const result = await runDigest(htmlSource, { outDir: join(process.cwd(), "test", "tmp-out"), client: { chat: async () => response } });
  const respText = await fs.promises.readFile(result.mdPath.replace(/\.md$/, ".response.txt"), "utf8");
  assert.strictEqual(respText, response);
  assert.ok(respText.includes("<notes>") && respText.includes("<check>") && respText.includes("<final>"));
});

<script>
const BASE = "";
const MAP = {};
document.querySelectorAll('figure[data-n]').forEach((f)=>{
  if (f.querySelector('img, svg')) return;
  const n=f.dataset.n,img=new Image();img.loading='lazy';img.alt='Figure '+n;
  img.onerror=()=>{const a=document.createElement('a');a.href=BASE.replace(/\/$/,'')+'#'+f.dataset.a;a.textContent='Open Fig. '+n+' on arXiv';a.className='ui text-sm underline';a.style.color='var(--teal)';img.replaceWith(a);};
  img.src=MAP[n] ? new URL(MAP[n], BASE || window.location.origin).href : "";
  f.prepend(img);

<script>
/* Reading marker. R or a click on a sentence starts it; Up/Down (or k/j) move it; Esc or R stops it.
   Arrow keys scroll the page normally unless the marker is active. Runs after MathJax has typeset. */
(function(){
const BLK='main p:not(.cite),main li:not(.cite),main .item,main blockquote,main figcaption,main td';
const ALL=BLK+',main mjx-container[display="true"]';
const ABBR=/(?:\b(?:e\.g|i\.e|et al|etc|vs|cf|Figs?|Eqs?|Secs?|Tab|Refs?|Algs?|approx|resp|No)|\b[A-Z])\.$/;
const OBJ='\uFFFC';
let units=[],idx=-1,on=false,ready=false,hintEl;

function tokens(block){
  const items=[];let run=[];
  const flush=()=>{if(run.length)items.push({run});run=[];};
  const walk=n=>{
    for(let c=n.firstChild;c;c=c.nextSibling){
      if(c.nodeType===3){run.push({t:c});}
      else if(c.nodeType===1){
        const tag=c.tagName;
        if(tag==='MJX-CONTAINER'){
          if(c.getAttribute('display')==='true'){flush();items.push({disp:c});}
          else run.push({a:c});
        }else if(tag==='SCRIPT'||tag==='STYLE'||tag==='MJX-ASSISTIVE-MML'||tag==='IMG'){}
        else walk(c);
      }
    }
  };
  walk(block);flush();return items;
}

function sentences(run){
  let s='';const map=[];
  for(const p of run){
    if(p.t){const v=p.t.nodeValue;for(let i=0;i<v.length;i++)map.push({n:p.t,o:i,L:v.length});s+=v;}
    else{map.push({a:p.a});s+=OBJ;}
  }
  const cuts=[0];
  const re=/[.!?]+["'’”)\]]*(?=\s+[A-Z0-9"'‘(\[\uFFFC])/g;
  let m;
  while((m=re.exec(s))){const end=m.index+m[0].length;if(!ABBR.test(s.slice(Math.max(0,end-10),end)))cuts.push(end);}
  cuts.push(s.length);
  const out=[];
  for(let i=0;i<cuts.length-1;i++){
    let a=cuts[i],b=cuts[i+1];
    while(a<b&&/\s/.test(s[a]))a++;
    while(b>a&&/\s/.test(s[b-1]))b--;
    if(b>a)out.push([a,b]);
  }
  return {out,map};
}

function rangeFor(block,map,a,b){
  const r=document.createRange(),f=map[a],l=map[b-1];
  if(f.a)r.setStartBefore(f.a);
  else if(f.o===0){let n=f.n;while(n.parentNode!==block&&n===n.parentNode.firstChild)n=n.parentNode;r.setStartBefore(n);}
  else r.setStart(f.n,f.o);
  if(l.a)r.setEndAfter(l.a);
  else if(l.o===l.L-1){let n=l.n;while(n.parentNode!==block&&n===n.parentNode.lastChild)n=n.parentNode;r.setEndAfter(n);}
  else r.setEnd(l.n,l.o+1);
  return r;
}

function build(block){
  const out=[];
  for(const it of tokens(block)){
    if(it.disp){it.disp.classList.add('rd');out.push(it.disp);continue;}
    const {out:ss,map}=sentences(it.run),spans=[];
    for(let i=ss.length-1;i>=0;i--){
      const r=rangeFor(block,map,ss[i][0],ss[i][1]);
      const sp=document.createElement('span');sp.className='rd';
      sp.appendChild(r.extractContents());r.insertNode(sp);spans.push(sp);
    }
    out.push(...spans.reverse());
  }
  return out;
}

function hint(){
  if(!hintEl){hintEl=document.createElement('div');hintEl.id='rd-hint';document.body.appendChild(hintEl);}
  hintEl.textContent=on?'\u2191 \u2193 move \u00b7 Esc exit':'R: reading marker';
}
function inView(i){const r=units[i].getBoundingClientRect();return r.bottom>0&&r.top<innerHeight;}
function firstVisible(){for(let i=0;i<units.length;i++)if(units[i].getBoundingClientRect().top>=48)return i;return units.length-1;}
function ensure(u,fast){
  const r=u.getBoundingClientRect(),h=innerHeight;
  if(r.top<h*.18||r.bottom>h*.78)scrollBy({top:r.top-h*.38,behavior:fast?'auto':'smooth'});
}
function set(i,scroll,fast){
  if(units[idx])units[idx].classList.remove('on');
  idx=Math.max(0,Math.min(units.length-1,i));
  units[idx].classList.add('on');
  if(scroll)ensure(units[idx],fast);
}
function activate(i){
  on=true;document.body.classList.add('rd-active');
  set(i!==undefined?i:(idx>=0&&inView(idx)?idx:firstVisible()),i===undefined);hint();
}
function deactivate(){
  on=false;document.body.classList.remove('rd-active');
  if(units[idx])units[idx].classList.remove('on');hint();
}

document.addEventListener('keydown',e=>{
  if(!units.length||e.ctrlKey||e.metaKey||e.altKey)return;
  const t=e.target;if(t&&(/^(INPUT|TEXTAREA|SELECT)$/.test(t.tagName)||t.isContentEditable))return;
  if(e.key==='r'||e.key==='R'){e.preventDefault();on?deactivate():activate();return;}
  if(!on)return;
  if(e.key==='ArrowDown'||e.key==='j'){e.preventDefault();set(idx+1,true,e.repeat);}
  else if(e.key==='ArrowUp'||e.key==='k'){e.preventDefault();set(idx-1,true,e.repeat);}
  else if(e.key==='Escape')deactivate();
});
document.addEventListener('click',e=>{
  const sel=getSelection();if(sel&&!sel.isCollapsed)return;
  if(!units.length||e.target.closest('a,button,nav'))return;
  const u=e.target.closest('.rd');if(!u)return;
  const i=units.indexOf(u);if(i<0)return;
  if(on){set(i,false);}else activate(i);
});

function init(){
  if(ready)return;ready=true;
  for(const el of Array.from(document.querySelectorAll(ALL))){
    if(el.tagName==='MJX-CONTAINER'){if(!el.parentElement.closest(BLK)){el.classList.add('rd');units.push(el);}continue;}
    if(el.querySelector(BLK))continue;
    units.push(...build(el));
  }
  hint();
}
addEventListener('load',()=>{
  const p=window.MathJax&&window.MathJax.startup&&window.MathJax.startup.promise;
  Promise.resolve(p).then(init,init);
});
})();
</script>
</body>
</html>

=== tests/digest.test.js ===

/**
 * Tests for src/digest.js (digestSource) and src/prompt.js (extractFinal /
 * extractDraft / extractNotes).
 *
 * Output contract (TASK_PIPELINE.md I7):
 *  - digestSource falls back to <draft> when <final> is null
 *  - the full raw response is stored alongside the parsed digest
 *  - a warning is logged when Fixes to apply: N > 0 and there is no <final>
 */

import { test } from "node:test";
import { strict as assert } from "node:assert";
import { extractFinal, extractDraft, extractNotes } from "../src/prompt.js";
import { digestSource } from "../src/digest.js";

function sectionDoc(title, motivationLine) {
  return (
    "---\n" +
    `title: ${title}\n` +
    "authors: A\nvenue: v\nid: 1\nwhatRead: w\nverdict: v\noverallConfidence: 80\n" +
    "---\n\n" +
    `## Motivation (Confidence: 80%)\n${motivationLine}\n\n` +
    "## Core idea (Confidence: 80%)\ncore.\n\n" +
    "## Distinguishing factor (Confidence: 80%)\ndiff.\n\n" +
    "## Evaluation and conclusions (Confidence: 80%)\n**Result.** r. My read: m.\n\n" +
    "## In case you missed it (Confidence: 80%)\n- [pos] e.\n\n" +
    "## Cautious judgement (Confidence: 80%)\n" +
    "| Question | Answer | Confidence |\n" +
    "| --- | --- | --- |\n" +
    "| Fair comparison to external baselines? | y | 80% |\n" +
    "| Invents its own FoM? | n | 80% |\n" +
    "| Rewrite of an existing idea? | n | 80% |\n" +
    "| Reliable enough to reuse? | y | 80% |\n\n" +
    "## Sources and limits (Confidence: 80%)\ns.\n"
  );
}

test("prompt: extractFinal returns null when there is no <final> block", () => {
  assert.strictEqual(extractFinal(null), null);
  assert.strictEqual(extractFinal(undefined), null);
  assert.strictEqual(extractFinal("no blocks here"), null);
  assert.strictEqual(extractFinal("<draft>x</draft>"), null);
});

test("prompt: extractFinal takes the last complete <final> match and trims it", () => {
  assert.strictEqual(extractFinal("<final>  spaced  </final>"), "spaced");
  assert.strictEqual(
    extractFinal("<final>first</final> junk <final>second</final>"),
    "second",
  );
});

test("prompt: extractDraft returns null when there is no <draft> block", () => {
  assert.strictEqual(extractDraft(null), null);
  assert.strictEqual(extractDraft("<final>x</final>"), null);
});

test("prompt: extractDraft takes the last complete <draft> match and trims it", () => {
  assert.strictEqual(extractDraft("<draft>  spaced  </draft>"), "spaced");
  assert.strictEqual(
    extractDraft("<draft>first</draft> junk <draft>second</draft>"),
    "second",
  );
});

test("prompt: extractNotes returns null when absent", () => {
  assert.strictEqual(extractNotes(null), null);
  assert.strictEqual(extractNotes("nothing"), null);
  assert.strictEqual(
    extractNotes("<notes>ledger</notes>"),
    "ledger",
  );
});

const SOURCE = {
  paperText: "some paper text",
  figures: [],
  baseUrl: "https://arxiv.org/html/2610.17099v1",
  whatRead: "Full text via arXiv HTML",
};

function clientFor(content) {
  return { chat: async () => content };
}

test("digestSource: falls back to <draft> when <final> is absent", async () => {
  const response =
    "<notes>\nledger\n</notes>\n<draft>\n" +
    sectionDoc("Draft Title", "DRAFT-ONLY") +
    "</draft>\n<check>\nFixes to apply: 0\n</check>";
  const digest = await digestSource(SOURCE, { client: clientFor(response) });

  assert.strictEqual(digest.title, "Draft Title");
  assert.strictEqual(digest.sections.length, 7);
  assert.ok(digest.sections[0].content.includes("DRAFT-ONLY"));
  assert.strictEqual(
    digest.sections.map((s) => s.id).join(","),
    "mot,core,diff,eval,icymi,judge,src",
  );
});

test("digestSource: uses <final> when the check applied fixes", async () => {
  const response =
    "<notes>\nledger\n</notes>\n<draft>\n" +
    sectionDoc("Draft Title", "DRAFT-ONLY") +
    "</draft>\n<check>\nFixes to apply: 1\n</check>\n<final>\n" +
    sectionDoc("Final Title", "FINAL-ONLY") +
    "</final>";
  const digest = await digestSource(SOURCE, { client: clientFor(response) });

  assert.strictEqual(digest.title, "Final Title");
  assert.strictEqual(digest.sections.length, 7);
  assert.ok(digest.sections[0].content.includes("FINAL-ONLY"));
  assert.ok(!digest.sections[0].content.includes("DRAFT-ONLY"));
});

test("digestSource: stores the full raw response (all blocks)", async () => {
  const response =
    "<notes>\nledger\n</notes>\n<draft>\n" +
    sectionDoc("Draft Title", "DRAFT-ONLY") +
    "</draft>\n<check>\nFixes to apply: 0\n</check>";
  const digest = await digestSource(SOURCE, { client: clientFor(response) });

  assert.ok(digest.raw.includes("<notes>"), "raw stores <notes>");
  assert.ok(digest.raw.includes("<draft>"), "raw stores <draft>");
  assert.ok(digest.raw.includes("<check>"), "raw stores <check>");
});

test("digestSource: warns when Fixes to apply: N > 0 but no <final>", async () => {
  const response =
    "<draft>\n" + sectionDoc("Draft Title", "DRAFT-ONLY") + "</draft>\n<check>\nFixes to apply: 1\n</check>";
  const warns = [];
  const origWarn = console.warn;
  console.warn = (...args) => warns.push(args.join(" "));
  try {
    const digest = await digestSource(SOURCE, { client: clientFor(response) });
    // falling back to the draft must not block rendering
    assert.strictEqual(digest.title, "Draft Title");
  } finally {
    console.warn = origWarn;
  }
  assert.ok(
    warns.some((w) => /Fixes to apply/.test(w)),
    "expected a warning about fixes without a <final>",
  );
});

test("digestSource: a truncated response with no complete block throws", async () => {
  const response = "<draft>\n---\ntitle: T\nverdict: v\noverallConfidence: 80\n"; // never closed
  await assert.rejects(
    digestSource(SOURCE, { client: clientFor(response) }),
    /no <final> or <draft> block/,
  );
});

=== tests/fetch.test.js ===

/**
 * Tests for src/fetch.js (parseFigures, resolve) and the HTML text extractor.
 *
 * The extractor (extractTextFromHtml) currently lives in src/cli.js, not
 * src/fetch.js, as TASK_TESTING.md assumes. The extractor tests below
 * therefore import it from src/cli.js, and the fetch.js import gap is
 * documented explicitly rather than left implicit.
 *
 * parseFigures (src/fetch.js) is DOM-based (linkedom): real figures only,
 * never tables; entries are {n, dataA, caption, url, svg}, with the svg field
 * holding serialised inline <svg> markup (CSS custom properties resolved to
 * literal colours) and url holding an image/object URL resolved against baseUrl.
 */

import { test } from "node:test";
import { strict as assert } from "node:assert";
import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { join } from "node:path";
import { parseFigures } from "../src/fetch.js";
import { extractTextFromHtml } from "../src/cli.js";

const FIXTURES = join(import.meta.dirname, "..", "test", "fixtures");

function read(name) {
  return readFileSync(join(FIXTURES, name), "utf8");
}

function countOccurrences(haystack, needle) {
  let n = 0;
  let i = haystack.indexOf(needle);
  while (i !== -1) {
    n += 1;
    i = haystack.indexOf(needle, i + 1);
  }
  return n;
}

////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
// parseFigures (src/fetch.js)
////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

test("fetch.js exports parseFigures", () => {
  assert.strictEqual(typeof parseFigures, "function");
});

test(
  "parseFigures: localtrain fixture (fixture 1) yields exactly two figures, no tables, fallback figures",
  () => {
    const html = read("localtrain.html");
    const figures = parseFigures(html, "https://localhost/");

    assert.strictEqual(figures.length, 2, "exactly two figure entries on fixture 1");
    assert.deepStrictEqual(figures.map((f) => f.n), ["1", "2"]);
    assert.deepStrictEqual(figures.map((f) => f.dataA), ["S2.F1", "S3.F2"]);
    assert.ok(
      figures[0].caption.includes("One CNN layer and its local supervision"),
    );
    assert.ok(figures[1].caption.includes("Hardware organization"));

    // tables must never leak into the figure list
    assert.ok(
      figures.every((f) => !/table/i.test(f.caption || "")),
      "no table captions in figure list",
    );

    // figures with neither image nor SVG fall back (empty url and svg)
    assert.strictEqual(figures[0].url, "");
    assert.strictEqual(figures[0].svg, "");
    assert.strictEqual(figures[1].url, "");
    assert.strictEqual(figures[1].svg, "");
  },
);

test(
  "parseFigures: arxiv sample excludes tables, serialises inline SVG, resolves img/object URLs",
  () => {
    const html = read("arxiv-sample.html");
    const baseUrl = "https://arxiv.org/html/2610.17099";
    const figures = parseFigures(html, baseUrl);

    assert.strictEqual(figures.length, 3, "three real figures, no tables");
    assert.ok(
      figures.every((f) => !/TABLE/i.test(f.caption)),
      "no tables listed",
    );
    assert.ok(
      figures.every((f) => f.id !== "S3.T1" && f.id !== "S3.T2"),
      "no table ids",
    );

    // inline SVG: serialised markup with CSS custom properties resolved
    const f1 = figures.find((f) => f.dataA === "S2.F1");
    assert.ok(f1, "figure S2.F1 present");
    assert.ok(f1.svg.includes("<svg"), "inline SVG serialised into svg field");
    assert.ok(
      f1.svg.includes('fill="#0E6B73"'),
      "fill custom property resolved to literal colour",
    );
    assert.ok(
      f1.svg.includes('stroke="#16252C"'),
      "stroke custom property resolved",
    );
    assert.strictEqual(f1.url, "", "SVG figure has no image url");

    // <object data> resolved against baseUrl
    const f2 = figures.find((f) => f.dataA === "S3.F2");
    assert.ok(f2, "figure S3.F2 present");
    assert.strictEqual(f2.url, "https://arxiv.org/html/fig2.pdf", "object url resolved");

    // <img src> resolved against baseUrl
    const f3 = figures.find((f) => f.dataA === "S4.F3");
    assert.ok(f3, "figure S4.F3 present");
    assert.strictEqual(f3.url, "https://arxiv.org/html/fig3.png", "img url resolved");
  },
);

//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
// extractor: extractTextFromHtml (implemented in src/cli.js)
////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

test("extractor: arxiv-sample — title first, chrome scoped out, headings marked, tables as pipe rows", () => {
  const text = extractTextFromHtml(read("arxiv-sample.html"));

  assert.ok(
    text.startsWith("# Title: Sample Paper on Local Learning"),
    "title is first with heading marker: " + JSON.stringify(text.slice(0, 60)),
  );
  const beforeTitle = text.slice(0, text.indexOf("Title:"));
  assert.ok(
    !/Report GitHub Issue|Funder:|nonprofit/.test(beforeTitle),
    "no chrome before title",
  );
  const afterRefs = text.slice(text.indexOf("5 References"));
  assert.ok(
    !/Instructions for reporting errors|Report GitHub Issue/.test(afterRefs),
    "no chrome after references",
  );

  // headings with markers
  assert.ok(text.includes("## 1 Introduction"), "h2 heading marked");
  assert.ok(text.includes("## 5 References"), "references heading marked");

  // tables serialised as caption + pipe-separated rows
  assert.ok(text.includes("Table I: Comparison of methods."), "table caption");
  assert.ok(text.includes("| Method | Accuracy |"), "table header row");
  assert.ok(text.includes("| Ours | 76.57% |"), "table body row");
  assert.ok(text.includes("Table II: Ablation results."), "second table caption");
});

test("extractor: each <math> alttext appears once; rendered form dropped", () => {
  const html =
    "<article class='ltx_document'><p>Gain <math alttext='1.86 \\times'>1.86 times</math> and risk <math alttext='s \\ge 0'>s &ge; 0</math>.</p></article>";
  const out = extractTextFromHtml(html);

  assert.strictEqual(out.split("\\(1.86 \\times\\)").length - 1, 1, "first equation once");
  assert.strictEqual(out.split("\\(s \\ge 0\\)").length - 1, 1, "second equation once");
  assert.ok(!out.includes("1.86 times"), "rendered form dropped");
  assert.ok(!out.includes("&ge;"), "rendered form dropped");
});

test("extractor: localtrain — UTF-8 symbols survive (non-LaTeXML HTML falls back to <body>)", () => {
  const text = extractTextFromHtml(read("localtrain.html"));

  assert.ok(text.includes("# LocalTrain:"), "title present with marker");
  assert.ok(text.includes("×"), "multiplication sign survives");
  assert.ok(text.includes("±"), "plus-minus sign survives");
  const CHROME = ["Report GitHub Issue", "arXiv is now an independent nonprofit", "Funder:"];
  for (const chunk of CHROME) {
    assert.ok(!text.includes(chunk), `no arxiv chrome: ${chunk}`);
  }
});

//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
// Gap documentation: extractTextFromHtml is not exported from src/fetch.js
////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

test("extractTextFromHtml is implemented in src/cli.js, not exported from src/fetch.js", async () => {
  const fetchMod = await import("../src/fetch.js");
  assert.strictEqual(
    fetchMod.extractTextFromHtml,
    undefined,
    "extractTextFromHtml is not exported from src/fetch.js (it lives in src/cli.js)",
  );
});

=== tests/llm.test.js ===

/**
 * Tests for src/llm.js: extractMarkdown (frontmatter + body parsing).
 */

import { test } from "node:test";
import { strict as assert } from "node:assert";
import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { join } from "node:path";
import { extractMarkdown } from "../src/llm.js";

const FIXTURES = join(import.meta.dirname, "..", "test", "fixtures");
const handwritten = readFileSync(join(FIXTURES, "handwritten.md"), "utf8");

test("llm: extractMarkdown parses the handwritten fixture frontmatter and body", () => {
  const { metadata, body, raw, _salvaged } = extractMarkdown(handwritten);

  assert.strictEqual(_salvaged, false, "clean fixture is not salvaged");
  assert.strictEqual(metadata.title, "A Sample Paper for Digest Rendering Tests");
  assert.deepStrictEqual(metadata.authors, ["Jane Doe", "John Smith"]);
  assert.strictEqual(metadata.venue, "arXiv:2601.00001v1 [cs.AR]");
  assert.strictEqual(metadata.id, "2601.00001");
  assert.strictEqual(metadata.overallConfidence, "80");
  // frontmatter keys are in the documented order
  assert.deepStrictEqual(
    Object.keys(metadata),
    ["title", "authors", "venue", "id", "whatRead", "verdict", "overallConfidence"],
  );
  assert.ok(body.includes("## Motivation (Confidence: 85%)"), "body begins with sections");
  assert.strictEqual(raw, handwritten.trim(), "raw is the normalised document");
});

test("llm: frontmatter scalar values with commas round-trip verbatim", () => {
  const md =
    "---\ntitle: T\nauthors: A, B\nvenue: v\nid: 1\n" +
    "whatRead: no code, no supplementary material\n" +
    "verdict: X, and Y, but not Z.\noverallConfidence: 80\n---\n\n" +
    "## Motivation (Confidence: 80%)\n\nmot body.\n";
  const { metadata } = extractMarkdown(md);

  assert.strictEqual(typeof metadata.verdict, "string", "verdict stays a scalar");
  assert.strictEqual(metadata.verdict, "X, and Y, but not Z.", "verdict round-trips verbatim");
  assert.strictEqual(
    metadata.whatRead,
    "no code, no supplementary material",
    "whatRead round-trips verbatim",
  );
});

test("llm: authors split on comma and re-join with \", \"", () => {
  const md =
    "---\ntitle: T\nauthors: Jane Doe, John Smith\n---\n\n" +
    "## Motivation (Confidence: 80%)\n\nx.\n";
  const { metadata } = extractMarkdown(md);

  assert.deepStrictEqual(metadata.authors, ["Jane Doe", "John Smith"]);
  assert.strictEqual(metadata.authors.join(", "), "Jane Doe, John Smith");
});

=== tests/pipeline.test.js ===

/**
 * End-to-end pipeline test with all HTTP spoofed via nock: no network.
 *
 * Spoofs an arXiv HTML fetch and an OpenRouter chat/completions response,
 * then runs resolve -> digestSource -> renderString.
 */

import { test } from "node:test";
import { strict as assert } from "node:assert";
import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { join } from "node:path";
import nock from "nock";
import { resolve } from "../src/fetch.js";
import { digestSource, createClient } from "../src/digest.js";
import { renderString } from "../src/template.js";

const FIXTURES = join(import.meta.dirname, "..", "test", "fixtures");

function read(name) {
  return readFileSync(join(FIXTURES, name), "utf8");
}

function sectionDoc(title, motivationLine) {
  return (
    "---\n" +
    `title: ${title}\n` +
    "authors: Tester One, Tester Two\nvenue: arXiv:2610.17099v1 [cs.AR]\n" +
    "id: 2610.17099\nwhatRead: Full text via arXiv HTML (main body and references; no code, no supplementary material)\n" +
    "verdict: The end-to-end pipeline works, with commas, in the verdict.\noverallConfidence: 80\n" +
    "---\n\n" +
    `## Motivation (Confidence: 80%)\n${motivationLine}\n\n` +
    "## Core idea (Confidence: 80%)\nCore idea text.\n\n" +
    "## Distinguishing factor (Confidence: 80%)\nDiff text.\n\n" +
    "## Evaluation and conclusions (Confidence: 80%)\n" +
    "**Headline result.** Result text. My read: read text.\n\n" +
    "## In case you missed it (Confidence: 80%)\n- [pos] something positive.\n\n" +
    "## Cautious judgement (Confidence: 80%)\n" +
    "| Question | Answer | Confidence |\n" +
    "| --- | --- | --- |\n" +
    "| Fair comparison to external baselines? | yes | 80% |\n" +
    "| Invents its own FoM? | no | 80% |\n" +
    "| Rewrite of an existing idea? | no | 80% |\n" +
    "| Reliable enough to reuse? | yes | 80% |\n\n" +
    "## Sources and limits (Confidence: 80%)\nI read the full text.\n"
  );
}

test(
  "pipeline: nocked arXiv fetch + nocked OpenRouter digest -> rendered HTML, no network",
  async () => {
    process.env.OPENROUTER_API_KEY = "test-key";
    nock.cleanAll();
    nock.disableNetConnect();

    try {
      const arxivHtml = read("arxiv-sample.html");
      const arxiv = nock("https://arxiv.org")
        .get("/html/2610.17099")
        .reply(200, arxivHtml);

      const response =
        "<notes>\nEvidence ledger line.\n</notes>\n<draft>\n" +
        sectionDoc("Pipeline Test Digest", "Pipeline motivation.") +
        "</draft>\n<check>\nFixes to apply: 0\n</check>";
      let openRouterBody = null;
      const openRouter = nock("https://openrouter.ai")
        .post("/api/v1/chat/completions", (body) => {
          openRouterBody = body;
          return true;
        })
        .reply(200, {
          id: "gen-pipeline-test",
          model: "mock-model",
          choices: [{ message: { content: response } }],
        });

      // 1. fetch stage: arXiv HTML (spoofed)
      const source = await resolve("2610.17099");
      assert.strictEqual(source.kind, "arxiv");
      assert.strictEqual(source.value, "2610.17099");
      assert.strictEqual(source.baseUrl, "https://arxiv.org/html/2610.17099");
      assert.ok(source.html.includes("Sample Paper on Local Learning"), "fixture HTML fetched");
      assert.ok(Array.isArray(source.figures) && source.figures.length >= 2, "figures parsed");

      // 2. agent stage: OpenRouter (spoofed), draft fallback since the check is clean
      const digest = await digestSource(
        { ...source, paperText: source.html },
        { client: createClient({ model: "mock-model" }) },
      );
      assert.ok(openRouter.isDone(), "OpenRouter interceptor consumed");
      assert.ok(openRouterBody.model && typeof openRouterBody.model === "string", "request forwarded to a model: " + openRouterBody.model);
      assert.ok(arxiv.isDone(), "arXiv interceptor consumed");
      assert.strictEqual(digest.title, "Pipeline Test Digest");
      assert.deepStrictEqual(
        digest.sections.map((s) => s.id),
        ["mot", "core", "diff", "eval", "icymi", "judge", "src"],
      );
      // The full raw response (all blocks) is stored alongside the digest so
      // failures can be diagnosed. (I7: the contract wants all blocks here;
      // that storage is verified in tests/digest.test.js.)
      assert.ok(
        digest.raw.includes("## Motivation"),
        "raw response stored (draft document)",
      );

      // 3. render stage: self-contained HTML, no network
      const rendered = await renderString(digest, {
        figures: source.figures,
        baseUrl: source.baseUrl,
      });
      assert.ok(rendered.includes("Pipeline Test Digest"), "title in rendered HTML");
      assert.ok(rendered.includes('id="mot"'), "Motivation section rendered");
      assert.ok(rendered.includes('id="src"'), "Sources section rendered");
      assert.ok(
        rendered.includes('const BASE = "https://arxiv.org/html/2610.17099";'),
        "baseUrl flows into the page script",
      );
    } finally {
      nock.enableNetConnect();
      nock.cleanAll();
      delete process.env.OPENROUTER_API_KEY;
    }
  },
);

=== tests/render.test.js ===

/**
 * Tests for src/render.js + src/template.js: Markdown digest -> HTML rendering.
 *
 * Structural invariants from TASK_PIPELINE.md:
 *  - each of the seven section bodies appears exactly once (I3)
 *  - Evaluation renders as four .item blocks with no .prose wrapper (I4)
 *  - commas in frontmatter keep their following spaces (I5)
 *  - math placeholders (I6) and figure pipeline (I2 render side)
 */

import { test } from "node:test";
import { strict as assert } from "node:assert";
import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { join } from "node:path";
import { extractMarkdown } from "../src/llm.js";
import { splitSections, parseIcymi, parseJudge } from "../src/digest.js";
import { render } from "../src/render.js";
import { renderString } from "../src/template.js";

const FIXTURES = join(import.meta.dirname, "..", "test", "fixtures");

function read(name) {
  return readFileSync(join(FIXTURES, name), "utf8");
}

function countOccurrences(haystack, needle) {
  let n = 0;
  let i = haystack.indexOf(needle);
  while (i !== -1) {
    n += 1;
    i = haystack.indexOf(needle, i + 1);
  }
  return n;
}

const handwritten = read("handwritten.md");
const { metadata, body } = extractMarkdown(handwritten);
const sections = splitSections(body);
// The pipeline attaches section-specific parsed data before rendering.
for (const s of sections) {
  if (s.id === "icymi") s.entries = parseIcymi(s.content);
  if (s.id === "judge") s.rows = parseJudge(s.content);
}
const digest = { ...metadata, sections, raw: handwritten };
const html = await renderString(digest, { figures: [], baseUrl: "" });

test("render: handwritten fixture parses into metadata plus seven sections", () => {
  assert.strictEqual(metadata.title, "A Sample Paper for Digest Rendering Tests");
  assert.deepStrictEqual(metadata.authors, ["Jane Doe", "John Smith"]);
  assert.strictEqual(metadata.venue, "arXiv:2601.00001v1 [cs.AR]");
  assert.strictEqual(metadata.id, "2601.00001");
  assert.strictEqual(metadata.overallConfidence, "80");
  assert.strictEqual(sections.length, 7);
  assert.deepStrictEqual(
    sections.map((s) => s.id),
    ["mot", "core", "diff", "eval", "icymi", "judge", "src"],
  );
});

test("render: each of the seven section bodies appears exactly once (structural invariant)", () => {
  const needles = {
    mot: "small seed set",
    core: "local classifier to each region",
    diff: "regional error expansion and tile folding",
    eval: "headline numbers and they are reproducible within the paper",
    icymi: "lookup tables and a shared reciprocal",
    judge: "power boundaries differ",
    src: "I did not read the appendix",
  };
  for (const [id, needle] of Object.entries(needles)) {
    const section = sections.find((s) => s.id === id);
    assert.ok(section, `section ${id} exists`);
    assert.strictEqual(
      countOccurrences(html, needle),
      1,
      `body of section ${id} rendered exactly once`,
    );
  }
  // every section heading renders exactly once
  for (const id of ["mot", "core", "diff", "eval", "icymi", "judge", "src"]) {
    assert.strictEqual(
      countOccurrences(html, `<h2 class="s" id="${id}"`),
      1,
      `heading ${id} rendered exactly once`,
    );
  }
});

test("render: evaluation section is four .item blocks with no .prose wrapper", () => {
  const evalStart = html.indexOf('<h2 class="s" id="eval"');
  assert.ok(evalStart !== -1, "Evaluation heading present");
  const nextH = html.indexOf("<h2", evalStart + 1);
  const region = nextH !== -1 ? html.slice(evalStart, nextH) : html.slice(evalStart);
  assert.strictEqual(countOccurrences(region, 'class="item"'), 4, "four .item blocks in Evaluation");
  assert.strictEqual(countOccurrences(region, 'class="prose"'), 0, "no .prose wrapper in Evaluation");
});

test("render: commas in frontmatter keep their following spaces", () => {
  assert.ok(
    html.includes(", and the results are promising"),
    "verdict comma+space preserved",
  );
  assert.ok(
    html.includes("dataset, so the generalizability"),
    "verdict comma+space preserved (2)",
  );
  assert.ok(
    html.includes("no code, no supplementary material"),
    "whatRead comma+space preserved",
  );
  assert.ok(!html.includes(",and the results"), "no comma-without-space in verdict");
  assert.ok(!html.includes("no code,no supplementary"), "no comma-without-space in whatRead");
});

test("render: frontmatter placeholders keep scalar values verbatim", () => {
  const commaDigest = {
    title: "T",
    authors: [],
    venue: "",
    id: "",
    whatRead: "no code, no supplementary material",
    verdict: "The paper is good, and thorough, but limited.",
    overallConfidence: 0,
    sections: [],
    raw: "",
  };
  const { placeholders } = render(commaDigest, {});
  assert.strictEqual(
    placeholders["{{VERDICT}}"],
    "The paper is good, and thorough, but limited.",
  );
  assert.strictEqual(
    placeholders["{{WHAT_READ}}"],
    "no code, no supplementary material",
  );
});

test("render: baseUrl flows into the page script as BASE", async () => {
  const out = await renderString(digest, {
    figures: [],
    baseUrl: "https://arxiv.org/html/2610.17099v1",
  });
  assert.ok(out.includes('const BASE = "https://arxiv.org/html/2610.17099v1";'), "BASE set");
});

test("render: math placeholders — display $$ -> \\[...\\], inline \\(...\\) passthrough, multi-line \\\\ kept", () => {
  assert.ok(html.includes("\\[Z = F V"), "display math converted to \\[...\\]");
  assert.ok(
    html.includes("\\mathrm{pre}"),
    "inline math passed through as \\(...\\) (\\mathrm{pre} appears in inline \(E_F = E V_\\mathrm{pre}\))",
  );
  assert.ok(html.includes("\\begin{aligned}"), "multi-line display equation content kept");
  assert.ok(html.includes("\\\\"), "multi-line display equation line breaks kept");
});

test("render: < and & inside restored math are escaped", async () => {
  const mathDigest = {
    title: "Math escaping",
    authors: [],
    venue: "",
    id: "",
    whatRead: "",
    verdict: "",
    overallConfidence: 0,
    sections: [
      { id: "core", title: "Core idea", content: "Range: \\(x < y \\& z\\)." },
    ],
    raw: "",
  };
  const out = await renderString(mathDigest, {});
  // esc() is applied to restored math: & -> &amp; (the LaTeX backslash before
  // it is preserved; MathJax reads the unescaped text from the DOM after
  // entity parsing, i.e. the original \(x < y \& z\)).
  assert.ok(
    out.includes("\\(x &lt; y \\&amp; z\\)"),
    "< and & escaped inside restored math",
  );
  assert.ok(
    !out.includes("\\(x < y \\& z\\)"),
    "raw < not left inside restored math",
  );
});

function figDigest(content) {
  return {
    title: "Figures",
    authors: [],
    venue: "",
    id: "",
    whatRead: "",
    verdict: "",
    overallConfidence: 0,
    sections: [{ id: "core", title: "Core idea", content }],
    raw: "",
  };
}

test("render: raster figure image is inlined into <figure> from source map", async () => {
  const out = await renderString(
    figDigest('<figure data-n="1"><figcaption>Fig. 1 caption</figcaption></figure>'),
    {
      figures: [{ n: 1, dataA: "S2.F1", caption: "Fig. 1", url: "https://cdn.example/fig1.png" }],
      baseUrl: "",
    },
  );
  assert.ok(
    out.includes('<img loading="lazy" alt="Figure 1" src="https://cdn.example/fig1.png">'),
    "raster image inlined",
  );
});

test("render: figure without image or SVG gets baseUrl so the client fallback link begins with https://arxiv.org/html/", async () => {
  const out = await renderString(
    figDigest('<figure data-n="1" data-a="S9.F1"><figcaption>Fig. 9 caption</figcaption></figure>'),
    {
      figures: [{ n: 1, dataA: "S9.F1", caption: "Fig. 9" }],
      baseUrl: "https://arxiv.org/html/2610.17099v1",
    },
  );
  // No server-side image is injected for a figure with neither image nor SVG;
  // the client-side script builds the fallback link from BASE + # + data-a.
  assert.ok(out.includes('const BASE = "https://arxiv.org/html/2610.17099v1";'), "BASE set");
  assert.ok(out.includes('data-a="S9.F1"'), "figure keeps its data-a id");
});

test("render: inline SVG figure is inlined into <figure>", async () => {
  const svg = '<svg width="400" height="200"><rect width="400" height="200"/></svg>';
  const out = await renderString(
    figDigest('<figure data-n="1" data-a="S2.F1"><figcaption>Fig. 1 caption</figcaption></figure>'),
    {
      figures: [{ n: 1, dataA: "S2.F1", caption: "Fig. 1", svg }],
      baseUrl: "",
    },
  );
  // scaleSvg normalises the <svg> tag (width 100%, viewBox from width/height)
  assert.ok(out.includes("<svg"), "inline SVG markup inlined into figure");
  assert.ok(out.includes('viewBox="0 0 400 200"'), "SVG viewBox normalised");
  assert.ok(out.includes('width="100%"'), "SVG scaled to column width");
});

<script>
/* ---------- Pure render (same logic as src/render.js) ---------- */
const ESC = { "&": "&amp;", "<": "&lt;", ">": "&gt;", '"': "&quot;" };
function esc(s) { return String(s == null ? "" : s).replace(/[&<>"']/g, c => ESC[c] || c); }
function normalizeLatex(s) { const t=String(s);const out=[];const DELIM="[(){}_]";for(let i=0;i<t.length;i++){const c=t[i];if(c==="\\"&&i+2<t.length&&t[i+1]==="\\"&&DELIM.includes(t[i+2])){out.push("\\",t[i+2]);i+=2}else{out.push(c)}}return out.join(""); }
function stripMathDelimiters(s) { return String(s).replace(/^\\\[\s*/, "").replace(/\s*\\\]$/, "").replace(/^\\\(\s*/, "").replace(/\s*\\\)$/, ""); }
function confidenceChip(conf) { return `<span class="conf">${conf == null ? 0 : Number(conf)}%</span>`; }
function chipSign(tag) { return { pos: "+", neg: "−", neu: "·" }[tag] || "·"; }
function renderSection(s) {
  const conf = confidenceChip(s.confidence);
  let body = "";
  switch (s.id) {
    case "mot": case "diff": case "core": case "eval":
      body = `<p>${esc(normalizeLatex(s.content || ""))}</p>`;
      if (s.id === "core") {
        if (s.equations?.length) body += s.equations.map(e => { const clean = stripMathDelimiters(normalizeLatex(e)); return `<p>\\[${esc(normalizeLatex(clean))}\\]</p>`; }).join("");
        if (s.figureId) body += `<figure data-n="${esc(String(s.figureId))}" data-a="${esc(String(s.figureId))}"><figcaption>Framework figure (${esc(String(s.figureId))}).</figcaption></figure>`;
      }
      if (s.id === "eval") {
        if (s.results?.length) body += `<ul class="b">` + s.results.map(r => `<li class="item">${esc(r)}</li>`).join("") + `</ul>`;
        if (s.conclusion) body += `<p class="item">${esc(s.conclusion)}</p>`;
      }
      break;
    case "icymi":
      body = (s.entries || []).map(e => `<div class="item"><span class="chip ${esc(e.tag || "neu")}">${chipSign(e.tag)}</span>${esc(e.text)}</div>`).join("");
      break;
    case "judge":
      body = `<div class="wrap"><table><thead><tr><th>Question</th><th>Answer</th><th>Conf.</th></tr></thead><tbody>` + (s.rows || []).map(r => `<tr><td>${esc(r.question)}</td><td>${esc(r.answer)}</td><td>${r.conf == null ? "" : r.conf}%</td></tr>`).join("") + `</tbody></table></div>`;
      break;
    case "src":
      body = `<p>${esc(s.content || "")}</p>`;
      break;
    default:
      body = `<p>${esc(s.content || "")}</p>`;
  }
  return `<h2 class="s" id="${esc(s.id)}">${esc(s.title)}${conf}</h2>${body}`;
}

function buildTemplate(digest, source = {}) {
  const figures = Array.isArray(source.figures) ? source.figures : [];
  const base = source.baseUrl || "";
  const map = {};
  figures.forEach((f, i) => { const key = String(f.n != null ? f.n : i + 1); map[key] = f.src || f.dataA || ""; });
  const mapJson = JSON.stringify(map);
  const navLinks = (digest.sections || []).map(s => `<a class="block py-1" href="#${esc(s.id)}">${esc(s.title)}</a>`).join("");
  const sectionsHtml = (digest.sections || []).map(s => renderSection(s)).join("");
  const citations = (((digest.sections || []).find(s => s.id === "src") || {}).citations || []).map(c => `<p class="cite">${esc(c)}</p>`).join("");
  const mapJs = Object.entries(map).map(([k, v]) => `    ${JSON.stringify(k)}: ${JSON.stringify(v)},`).join("\n");
  let html = templateHtml;
  for (const [token, value] of Object.entries({
    "{{TITLE}}": esc(digest.title || ""),
    "{{AUTHORS}}": esc((digest.authors || []).join(", ")),
    "{{VENUE}}": esc(digest.venue || ""),
    "{{ID}}": esc(digest.id || ""),
    "{{WHAT_READ}}": esc(digest.whatRead || ""),
    "{{VERDICT}}": esc(digest.verdict || ""),
    "{{OVERALL_CONF}}": esc(digest.overallConfidence || 0),
    "{{SECTIONS}}": sectionsHtml,
    "{{CITATIONS}}": citations,
    "{{NAV_LINKS}}": navLinks,
    "{{BASE}}": esc(base),
    "{{MAP}}": mapJson,
  })) html = html.split(token).join(value);
  html = html.replace("const MAP = {{MAP}};", `const MAP = {\n${mapJs}\n  };`);
  return html;
}

const templateHtml = `<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
<meta charset="utf-8">
<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">
<title>Digest: {{TITLE}}</title>
<link rel="preconnect" href="https://fonts.googleapis.com">
<link href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=Literata:opsz,wght@7..72,400;7..72,600&family=Instrument+Sans:wght@400;600&display=swap" rel="stylesheet">
<script src="https://cdn.tailwindcss.com/3.4.17"></script>
<script>window.MathJax={tex:{inlineMath:[['\\\(','\\\)']],displayMath:[['\\\\[','\\\\]']]},svg:{fontCache:'global'}};</script>
<script async src="https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/mathjax@3.2.2/es5/tex-svg.js"></script>
<style>
:root{--paper:#F2F5F3;--ink:#16252C;--mute:#5B6C73;--rule:#CBD6D3;--teal:#0E6B73;--gain:#2B7A4B;--risk:#A83A2C;--warn:#9A6A12;--card:#FBFCFB}

.chip{font-family:'Instrument Sans',system-ui,sans-serif;font-size:.72rem;font-weight:600;border-radius:4px;padding:.05rem .4rem;margin-right:.4rem;color:#fff}

.wrap{overflow-x:auto}
blockquote{border-left:3px solid var(--rule);padding-left:1rem;color:var(--mute)}

figure{background:var(--card);border:1px solid var(--rule);padding:.6rem;width:100%;max-width:72ch}

main .cite,main .conf{text-align:left;hyphens:none}
main p{margin:.75rem 0}
main blockquote{margin:1.25rem 0}
main figure{margin:1.5rem 0}
mjx-container[jax="SVG"]{display:inline-block!important;overflow:visible}
mjx-container[jax="SVG"] svg{display:inline-block!important}
mjx-container[jax="SVG"][display="true"]{display:flex!important;justify-content:safe center;overflow-x:auto;overflow-y:hidden;max-width:100%;margin:1rem 0}
.rd.on{background:rgba(22,37,44,.15);box-shadow:0 0 0 2px rgba(22,37,44,.15);border-radius:3px;box-decoration-break:clone;-webkit-box-decoration-break:clone}
body.rd-active .rd:hover:not(.on){background:rgba(22,37,44,.06)}
#rd-hint{position:fixed;right:.8rem;bottom:.7rem;font:600 .7rem 'Instrument Sans',system-ui,sans-serif;color:var(--mute);background:var(--card);border:1px solid var(--rule);border-radius:6px;padding:.2rem .55rem;opacity:.9;pointer-events:none}
@media print{.rd.on{background:none;box-shadow:none}#rd-hint{display:none}}
</style>

const BASE = "{{BASE}}";
